/* Additive, isolated fixture picker for the connected score-model pipeline.
   Existing live-feed controls remain owned by exact-score-intelligence.js. */
(function () {
  "use strict";
  const $ = (id) => document.getElementById(id);
  const button = $("ufiPipelineLoadFixtures");
  const picker = $("ufiPipelineFixturePicker");
  const baseInput = $("esiApiBase");
  const dateInput = $("esiFeedDate");
  const idInput = $("esiProviderFixtureId");
  if (!button || !picker || !idInput) return;

  const DEFAULT_API = "https://universal-football-intelligence.onrender.com";
  if (baseInput && !baseInput.value) baseInput.value = DEFAULT_API;
  if (dateInput && !dateInput.value) {
    const now = new Date();
    dateInput.value = [now.getFullYear(), String(now.getMonth() + 1).padStart(2, "0"), String(now.getDate()).padStart(2, "0")].join("-");
  }

  function apiBase() {
    const value = String((baseInput && baseInput.value) || DEFAULT_API).trim().replace(/\/+$/, "");
    if (!/^https:\/\//i.test(value)) throw new Error("Enter the HTTPS URL of the deployed UFI backend.");
    return value;
  }

  async function requestJson(path) {
    const response = await fetch(apiBase() + path, {
      method: "GET",
      headers: { Accept: "application/json" },
      cache: "no-store"
    });
    let payload;
    try { payload = await response.json(); }
    catch (_) { throw new Error("Backend returned a non-JSON response (HTTP " + response.status + ")."); }
    if (!response.ok || payload.ok === false) throw new Error(payload.error || ("Backend returned HTTP " + response.status + "."));
    return payload;
  }

  function rowsFrom(payload) {
    let value = payload;
    for (let i = 0; i < 6 && value && typeof value === "object"; i++) {
      if (Array.isArray(value)) return value;
      if (Array.isArray(value.data)) return value.data;
      value = value.data;
    }
    return [];
  }

  function fixtureName(fixture) {
    if (typeof fixture.name === "string" && fixture.name.trim()) return fixture.name.trim();
    let home = "", away = "";
    (Array.isArray(fixture.participants) ? fixture.participants : []).forEach((p) => {
      const side = String((p && p.meta && p.meta.location) || "").toLowerCase();
      if (side === "home") home = String(p.name || "").trim();
      if (side === "away") away = String(p.name || "").trim();
    });
    return home && away ? home + " vs " + away : "Fixture " + String(fixture.id);
  }

  function make(tag, text) {
    const node = document.createElement(tag);
    if (text !== undefined) node.textContent = String(text);
    return node;
  }

  function render(payload) {
    const fixtures = rowsFrom(payload).filter((f) => f && f.id !== undefined && /^\d+$/.test(String(f.id)));
    picker.replaceChildren();
    if (!fixtures.length) {
      picker.textContent = "No usable source fixtures returned for " + (dateInput && dateInput.value || "the selected date") + ". Check provider coverage or try another date.";
      return;
    }
    const intro = make("p", "Loaded " + fixtures.length + " source fixture(s) from " + String(payload.source || "the configured provider") + ". Select one to populate the provider ID for the model pipeline.");
    picker.appendChild(intro);
    const list = make("div");
    list.className = "button-row";
    fixtures.forEach((fixture) => {
      const id = String(fixture.id);
      const name = fixtureName(fixture);
      const kickoff = fixture.starting_at || fixture.kickoff || fixture.commence_time || "Kickoff unknown";
      const choose = make("button", name + " · " + kickoff + " · ID " + id);
      choose.type = "button";
      choose.className = "button";
      choose.dataset.ufiPipelineFixtureId = id;
      choose.addEventListener("click", () => {
        idInput.value = id;
        const participants = Array.isArray(fixture.participants) ? fixture.participants : [];
        participants.forEach((p) => {
          const side = String((p && p.meta && p.meta.location) || "").toLowerCase();
          const team = String((p && p.name) || "").trim();
          if (side === "home" && $("esiHome")) $("esiHome").value = team;
          if (side === "away" && $("esiAway")) $("esiAway").value = team;
        });
        picker.querySelectorAll("[data-ufi-pipeline-fixture-id]").forEach((el) => { el.setAttribute("aria-pressed", String(el === choose)); });
        const pipelineStatus = $("ufiPipelineStatus");
        if (pipelineStatus) pipelineStatus.textContent = "Selected " + name + " (provider fixture " + id + "). Choose the matching historical league code, then run the connected pipeline.";
      });
      choose.setAttribute("aria-pressed", "false");
      list.appendChild(choose);
    });
    picker.appendChild(list);
  }

  button.addEventListener("click", async () => {
    const day = String((dateInput && dateInput.value) || "").trim();
    if (!/^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$/.test(day)) {
      picker.textContent = "Choose a valid fixture date first.";
      return;
    }
    button.disabled = true;
    button.textContent = "Loading source fixtures…";
    picker.textContent = "Fetching fixtures for " + day + " from the configured UFI backend…";
    try {
      render(await requestJson("/api/fixtures?date=" + encodeURIComponent(day)));
    } catch (error) {
      picker.textContent = "Fixture request failed: " + String(error && error.message || error) + ". The existing live-feed controls are unchanged.";
    } finally {
      button.disabled = false;
      button.textContent = "Load source fixtures for prediction";
    }
  });
})();

# UFI Model Brain — research-grounded operating rules

Purpose: a traceable research guide for Universal Football Intelligence (UFI). This file is not a claim that all sources were fully accessible or that the app already implements every method. It separates established mechanics from features requiring data and validation.

## Operating doctrine

1. Evidence provenance first. Every input must be labelled public source, user-entered note, computed output, model estimate, or unknown. Record data timestamps, source URLs, market definitions and exact fixture ID. OCR text is provisional until checked.
2. Model before narrative. An LLM may summarize, challenge assumptions and list what to verify. It must not invent odds, lineups, injuries, player foot preference, weather, tracking coordinates, xG or probabilities. Its answer cannot turn HOLD into a cleared bet.
3. Fit and test. Fit attack/defence strengths on historical scorelines with time decay; compare independent Poisson, Dixon-Coles and simple league/home-away baselines. Test on later unseen matches. Track log loss, multiclass Brier, ranked probability score, calibration and performance by league/market. Do not optimize on the same test period used to report performance.
4. Probability and price. Decimal odds imply 1/odds before overround correction. For an exact market only, EV per unit stake is p*odds - 1. If p is uncalibrated, odds missing, settlement unclear, or market differs, EV is not established. Account for dependence in accumulators; multiplying leg probabilities assumes independence and is often wrong.
5. Physics is a conditional layer, not magic. Drag, Reynolds number, surface roughness, spin and Magnus effects affect a ball's trajectory. They do not directly determine match results. A useful shot-level physics model needs measured launch speed/angle/spin, ball properties, wind and trajectory/shot context plus outcome data. Without this, do not add a made-up physics score.
6. Spatial and biomechanical layer. Pitch control needs time-stamped player and ball coordinates, velocities, acceleration/reaction assumptions and team context. Event coordinates are sparse event locations, not full optical tracking. Dominant/weak foot, height/weight, style and substitute impact must be source-backed and time-relevant; missing data stays unknown.
7. Context and news. Recent form must be opponent-adjusted and split by venue with adequate sample size. Injuries/suspensions/lineups need recent reliable reports; search headlines (including GDELT) are leads, not confirmation. Schedule congestion and motivation should be explicit hypotheses, not guessed narrative.
8. Risk gate. Hard-stop only for verifiable contradictions (e.g. wrong/settled/historical fixture, invalid odds or impossible input). Unknown evidence, model disagreement, stale prices or unverified news should generally HOLD. “No red flag found” does not mean safe.
9. Replacement discipline. Replacement picks require a current fixture, exact market/settlement, verified price, model estimate for that market, independent baseline comparison and calibrated out-of-sample evidence. Otherwise show “NO QUALIFIED REPLACEMENT”; never fill a 3-leg ticket just to satisfy a format.
10. Bankroll. Kelly is a stake-sizing rule, not a match predictor. Use only after a robust positive edge is demonstrated; estimation error, correlated legs and bookmaker limits argue for conservative fractional sizing. Never present a staking formula as a way to guarantee profit.

## Source review register
[Full source list from the original file is preserved in the GitHub version]

/* Universal Match State + All-Event Simulation + Low-Variance Trap Engine
 * Research prototype. Manual inputs only; no live data source is implied.
 */
(() => {
'use strict';
const $=id=>document.getElementById(id), clamp=(v,a,b)=>Math.max(a,Math.min(b,v));
const esc=s=>String(s??'').replace(/[&<>"']/g,c=>({'&':'&amp;','<':'&lt;','>':'&gt;','"':'&quot;',"'":'&#39;'}[c]));
const num=id=>Number($(id).value);
function readJson(id, fallback){try{const x=JSON.parse($(id).value||JSON.stringify(fallback));if(!x||typeof x!=='object')throw Error('Expected JSON object/array');return x}catch(e){throw Error(id+': '+e.message)}}
function poisson(k,l){let p=Math.exp(-l);for(let i=1;i<=k;i++)p*=l/i;return p}
function scores(h,a,lh,la,live){const out=[];for(let i=h;i<=h+8;i++)for(let j=a;j<=a+8;j++){out.push({h:i,a:j,p:poisson(i-h,lh)*poisson(j-a,la)})}const sum=out.reduce((s,x)=>s+x.p,0)||1;out.forEach(x=>x.p/=sum);return out.sort((x,y)=>y.p-x.p)}
function run(){
 const home=$('umeHome').value.trim()||'Home',away=$('umeAway').value.trim()||'Away',minute=clamp(num('umeMinute')||0,0,120);
 const live=$('umeLive').checked, curH=Math.max(0,Math.floor(num('umeScoreH')||0)),curA=Math.max(0,Math.floor(num('umeScoreA')||0));
 const state=readJson('umeState',{});
 const events=readJson('umeEvents',[]);
 if(!Array.isArray(events))throw Error('Event timeline must be a JSON array.');
 const hprof=state.home||{},aprof=state.away||{},env=state.environment||{},ref=state.referee||{};

 const notes=[],zoneRisk={home:0,away:0};let mds=0,drift=0,ss=0,reds=0,warning=false,woodwork=0,setpieces=0;
 // Structural baseline: low-block + dominant opposing possession suppresses high-danger expected goals.
 function stagnation(team,opponent){const low=String(team.block||'').toLowerCase()==='low';const poss=Number(opponent.possessionShare)||50;return low&&poss>=60?0.22:0}
 const stagH=stagnation(hprof,aprof),stagA=stagnation(aprof,hprof);
 lh*=1-stagH;la*=1-stagA;
 const ppdaH=Number(hprof.ppda),ppdaA=Number(aprof.ppda);
 const staminaH=clamp(Number(hprof.staminaDip80)||0,0,0.5),staminaA=clamp(Number(aprof.staminaDip80)||0,0,0.5);
 if(live&&minute>=60){lh*=1-staminaH*Math.min(1,(minute-60)/20);la*=1-staminaA*Math.min(1,(minute-60)/20)}
 // Environmental/referee factors are bounded and modest until empirically calibrated.
 const rain=clamp(Number(env.rain)||0,0,1),wind=clamp(Number(env.wind)||0,0,1),pitch=String(env.pitch||'normal').toLowerCase();
 const envFactor=clamp(1-0.08*rain-0.06*wind-(pitch==='poor'?0.08:0),0.65,1.05);lh*=envFactor;la*=envFactor;
 const strict=clamp(Number(ref.strictness)||0,0,1);let yellows={home:0,away:0};
 for(const e of events){
  const type=String(e.type||'').toLowerCase(),team=e.team==='away'?'away':'home',opp=team==='home'?'away':'home';
  const t=team==='home'?'H':'A',o=team==='home'?'A':'H';let level=1;
  if(['foul','yellow','minor_foul'].includes(type)){yellows[team]++;zoneRisk[team]+=.15;mds+=.08+strict*.05;notes.push('Level 1 · '+type+' ('+team+'): aggression and late-card risk updated.')}
  else if(['red','second_yellow','direct_red'].includes(type)){reds++;level=2;if(team==='home'){lh=Math.max(0,lh-.40);la=Math.min(6,la+.35)}else{la=Math.max(0,la-.40);lh=Math.min(6,lh+.35)}mds+=.6;notes.push('Level 2 · red card ('+team+'): attack and concession rates adjusted.')}
  else if(['manager_red','bench_red'].includes(type)){drift+=.10;mds+=.2;level=2;notes.push('Level 2 · bench/manager red: positioning drift increased.')}
  else if(['key_playmaker_out','playmaker_injury'].includes(type)){if(team==='home')lh*=.8;else la*=.8;level=2;notes.push('Level 2 · key playmaker out: transition velocity reduced 20%.')}
  else if(['keeper_injury','keeper_sub'].includes(type)){if(team==='home')la*=1.25;else lh*=1.25;level=2;notes.push('Level 2 · keeper change: opposing conversion factor +25% (experimental).')}
  else if(['attacking_sub','formation_attack'].includes(type)){if(team==='home')lh*=1.08;else la*=1.08;level=2;notes.push('Level 2 · attacking tactical switch: attacking branch widened.')}
  else if(['defensive_sub','formation_defend'].includes(type)){if(team==='home')lh*=.94;else la*=.94;level=2;notes.push('Level 2 · defensive switch: attack expectation reduced slightly.')}
  else if(['var_goal_cancelled','var_cancellation'].includes(type)){if(team==='home')la*=1.15;else lh*=1.15;mds+=.35;level=2;notes.push('Level 2 · VAR goal cancellation: opposing momentum +15% (10-minute heuristic).')}
  else if(type==='penalty_scored'){if(team==='home')lh=Math.max(lh,0)+.0;else la=Math.max(la,0)+.0;notes.push('Penalty scored: current score must be updated by the data operator.')}
  else if(type==='penalty_missed'){if(team==='home')la*=1.05;else lh*=1.05;mds+=.15;notes.push('Missed penalty: opposing momentum drift increased.')}
  else if(['controversial_call','officiating_delay','pitch_degradation'].includes(type)){mds+=.4;warning=true;level=3;notes.push('Level 3 · '+type+': virtual caution; check market divergence and reduce model weight.')}
  else if(type==='early_goal'){lh*=1.3;la*=1.3;level=2;notes.push('Level 2 · early goal: open-play factor ×1.3.')}
  else if(type==='woodwork'){woodwork++;mds+=.08;notes.push('Pressure spike: woodwork event counted.')}
  else if(type==='set_piece'){setpieces++;notes.push('Pressure spike: set-piece sequence counted.')}
  else if(type==='own_goal'||type==='defensive_howler'){if(team==='home')la=Math.max(la,0);else lh=Math.max(lh,0);mds+=.2;notes.push('Mechanical-defect mark: clean-sheet probability for the affected side is treated as zero for this phase; update current score if goal was awarded.')}
  else if(type==='non_dominant_foot_error'){mds+=.12;warning=true;notes.push('Pressing vulnerability metric increased after non-dominant-foot error.')}
  else if(type==='transition'){ss+=1}
  else if(type==='box_entry'){ss+=1}
  else if(type==='high_disruption'){mds+=.5;warning=true;level=3;notes.push('Level 3 · high disruption: odds divergence check recommended.')}
  if(e.critical===true||type==='multi_red_anomaly')mds+=1;
 }
 // Late desperation expands the trailing team's attacking branch and counter exposure.
 if(live&&minute>=75&&Math.abs(curH-curA)===1){if(curH<curA)lh*=1.15;else la*=1.15;mds+=.05;notes.push('Late desperation phase: trailing-side attack expectation +15% (heuristic).')}
 // Tempo / entropy / trap metrics use the operator-entered event evidence and profile fields.
 const transitions=Math.max(0,Number(state.transitions)||0),entries=Math.max(0,Number(state.finalThirdEntries)||0),progressive=Math.max(0,Number(state.progressivePasses)||0);
 const expectedTransitions=Math.max(1,Number(state.expectedTransitions)||8),expectedEntries=Math.max(1,Number(state.expectedFinalThirdEntries)||12);
 const tempoRate=(transitions+progressive*.15+entries*.2)/(expectedTransitions+expectedEntries*.2);
 const tsi=clamp(1-tempoRate,0,1),entropy=clamp(Number(state.entropy)||((mds+drift+woodwork*.08+setpieces*.03)/3),0,1);
 const lvts=clamp((stagH+stagA)/.44*.45+tsi*.35+(1-entropy)*.20,0,1);
 const tcs=clamp(mds+drift+reds*.45+zoneRisk.home+zoneRisk.away,0,10);
 const limit=Number($('umeChaosLimit').value)||2.5;
 const discarded=reds>=2||mds>=limit||tcs>=limit*1.8;
 const trap=lvts>=.68&&!discarded;
 if(trap){lh*=.78;la*=.78;warning=true;notes.push('TRAP GAME LOCKED: Low-Variance Sterile Environment Confirmed (Shifting to Tight Score Matrix).')}
 if(warning&&!discarded){lh*=.94;la*=.94}
 lh=clamp(lh,0,6);la=clamp(la,0,6);
 const top=scores(curH,curA,lh,la,live).slice(0,3);
 const green=!discarded&&!warning&&mds<.5&&tcs<limit&&stagH+stagA<.1;
 const flag=discarded?'MATCH DISCARDED: High Multi-Event Anomaly.':trap?'TRAP GAME LOCKED: Low-Variance Sterile Environment Confirmed (Shifting to Tight Score Matrix).':green?'UNIVERSAL SIMULATION APPROVED: Clean Multi-Outcome Verification.':'VIRTUAL CAUTION: Structural/event anomaly detected; reduce model weight and review data.';
 $('umeOutput').innerHTML='<div class="notice '+(discarded||warning?'warning':'good')+'"><b>'+esc(flag)+'</b></div>'+

 (discarded?'<p>Predictions suppressed by the Level 4 circuit breaker.</p>':'<h3>Top 3 final-score branches</h3><div class="table-wrap"><table><thead><tr><th>Rank</th><th>Final score</th><th>Model probability</th></tr></thead><tbody>'+top.map((x,i)=>'<tr><td>'+(i+1)+'</td><td><b>'+x.h+'–'+x.a+'</b></td><td>'+(x.p*100).toFixed(2)+'%</td></tr>').join('')+'</tbody></table></div>')+

}
$('umeRun').addEventListener('click',()=>{try{run()}catch(e){$('umeOutput').innerHTML='<div class="notice warning">'+esc(e.message)+'</div>'}});
function scoreFromFixture(f){
 const s=f?.score;
 if(s&&typeof s==='object'){
  const h=s.home??s.homeScore??s.home_score,a=s.away??s.awayScore??s.away_score;
  if(h!=null&&a!=null&&Number.isFinite(Number(h))&&Number.isFinite(Number(a)))return {h:Number(h),a:Number(a)};
 }
 if(f?.home_score!=null&&f?.away_score!=null&&Number.isFinite(Number(f.home_score))&&Number.isFinite(Number(f.away_score)))return {h:Number(f.home_score),a:Number(f.away_score)};
 if(Array.isArray(f?.scores)){
  const current=f.scores.filter(x=>/^(CURRENT|FT|FULL_TIME|FINAL)$/i.test(String(x?.description||x?.type?.name||'')));
  const rows=current.length?current:f.scores;
  const home=rows.find(x=>String(x?.score?.participant??x?.participant??'').toLowerCase()==='home');
  const away=rows.find(x=>String(x?.score?.participant??x?.participant??'').toLowerCase()==='away');
  const h=home?.score?.goals??home?.goals,a=away?.score?.goals??away?.goals;
  if(h!=null&&a!=null&&Number.isFinite(Number(h))&&Number.isFinite(Number(a)))return {h:Number(h),a:Number(a)};
 }
 return null;
}

          root = Path("output/openfootball")
          manifest_path = root / "manifest.json"
          csv_path = root / "matches.csv"
          if not manifest_path.is_file() or not csv_path.is_file():
              raise SystemExit("Expected manifest.json and matches.csv were not created.")

          manifest = json.loads(manifest_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
          with csv_path.open(encoding="utf-8-sig", newline="") as handle:
              reader = csv.DictReader(handle)
              required = {"date", "league_code", "home_team", "away_team", "home_goals", "away_goals"}
              missing = required - set(reader.fieldnames or [])
              if missing:
                  raise SystemExit(f"CSV is missing required columns: {sorted(missing)}")
              rows = list(reader)

          expected = int(manifest.get("match_rows", -1))
          if len(rows) != expected:
              raise SystemExit(f"CSV row count ({len(rows)}) does not match manifest ({expected}).")
          if not rows:
              raise SystemExit("No match rows were downloaded; refusing to mark dataset valid.")

          completed = sum(
              1 for row in rows
              if str(row.get("home_goals", "")).strip() != ""
              and str(row.get("away_goals", "")).strip() != ""
          )
          if completed == 0:
              raise SystemExit("No completed matches with scores found; backtest cannot run.")

          summary = [
              "## UFI OpenFootball dataset validation",
              "",
              f"- **Seasons requested:** {manifest.get('start_year')}–{manifest.get('end_year')}",
              f"- **League-season files found:** {manifest.get('files_found')}",
              f"- **Fixture/result rows:** {len(rows):,}",
              f"- **Rows with scores:** {completed:,}",
              f"- **Rows without scores:** {len(rows) - completed:,}",
              "- **Validation:** passed",
              "",
              "The source contains fixtures/results, not full player or tracking data."
          ]
          with open(os.environ["GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY"], "a", encoding="utf-8") as out:
              out.write("\n".join(summary) + "\n")
          print(f"Validated {len(rows)} rows; {completed} have scores across {manifest.get('files_found')} source files.")
          PY
      - name: Run chronological baseline backtest
        run: |
          python tools/openfootball_backtest.py \
            --input output/openfootball/matches.csv \
            --out output/openfootball/model
          python - <<'PY'
          import json
          import os
          from pathlib import Path

          metrics = json.loads(Path("output/openfootball/model/metrics.json").read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
          model = metrics["team_model"]
          baseline = metrics["league_frequency_baseline"]
          lines = [
              "",
              "## UFI chronological baseline backtest",
              "",
              f"- **Matches evaluated:** {metrics['backtested_matches']:,}",
              f"- **Team model 1X2 accuracy:** {model['accuracy_1x2']}",
              f"- **League baseline 1X2 accuracy:** {baseline['accuracy_1x2']}",
              f"- **Team model mean Brier score:** {model['mean_brier_1x2']}",
              f"- **League baseline mean Brier score:** {baseline['mean_brier_1x2']}",
              f"- **Team model mean log loss:** {model['mean_logloss_1x2']}",
              f"- **League baseline mean log loss:** {baseline['mean_logloss_1x2']}",
              f"- **Rows skipped:** {metrics['rows_skipped_for_missing_or_invalid_fields']}",
              f"- **Leakage control:** {metrics['same_date_leakage_control']}",
              "- **Per-league metrics:** available in output/openfootball/model/metrics.json",
              "- **Prediction records:** output/openfootball/model/chronological_predictions.csv",
              "",
              "Lower Brier score and log loss are better. These are research metrics, not proof of betting profitability."
          ]
          with open(os.environ["GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY"], "a", encoding="utf-8") as out:
              out.write("\n".join(lines) + "\n")
          PY
      - name: Prepare evidence templates and run integrated Phase 1–3 pipeline
        run: |
          mkdir -p output/openfootball/player-intelligence
          cp data/templates/player_registry_template.csv output/openfootball/player-intelligence/player_registry_template.csv
          cp data/templates/pre_match_odds_template.csv output/openfootball/player-intelligence/pre_match_odds_template.csv
          cp docs/player-intelligence-registry.md output/openfootball/player-intelligence/README.md
          python tools/ufi_integrated_pipeline.py \
            --matches output/openfootball/matches.csv \
            --out output/openfootball/integrated
          python - <<'PY'
          import json, os
          from pathlib import Path
          report = json.loads(Path("output/openfootball/integrated/ufi_integrated_report.json").read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
          p1, p2, p3 = report["phase_1_match_model"], report["phase_2_player_intelligence"], report["phase_3_odds_validation"]
          models = p1["models"]
          lines = [
              "", "## UFI integrated Phase 1–3 pipeline", "",
              f"- **Advanced model matches:** {p1['backtested_matches']:,}",
              f"- **Elo + Poisson ensemble accuracy:** {models.get('fixed_elo_plus_poisson_ensemble', {}).get('accuracy')}",
              f"- **Elo-only accuracy:** {models.get('elo_only', {}).get('accuracy')}",
              f"- **Historical baseline accuracy:** {models.get('league_frequency_baseline', {}).get('accuracy')}",
              f"- **Phase 2 status:** {p2['status']}",
              f"- **Sourced player records:** {p2['registry_audit']['records']}",
              f"- **Phase 3 status:** {p3['status']}",
              f"- **Odds status:** {p3['odds_status']}",
              f"- **Paper bets recorded:** {p3['paper_bet_count']}",
              f"- **Paper ROI:** {p3['paper_roi']}",
              "- **Leakage control:** all same-date fixtures are forecast before that date's results update the model.",
              "- **Integrated report:** output/openfootball/integrated/ufi_integrated_report.md and .json",
              "", "Player features remain disabled until sourced player-to-match participation data is verified. Historical accuracy is not proof of betting profitability."
          ]
          with open(os.environ["GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY"], "a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
              f.write("\n".join(lines) + "\n")
          PY

          mkdir -p backup-output/branch-snapshots

          Public URL: https://universal-football-intelligence.onrender.com

          Render branch: feature/5400-bookmaker-edge-gate

          Region: Singapore
          Plan: Free
          Auto-deploy: on commit
          Latest observed deploy commit (2026-10-28): 3de6e328c18f2136f214a396b078b0e6d242ee18
          Secret values are intentionally excluded. Back up/recreate them from Render Dashboard > service > Environment.
          This manifest cannot export Render's live filesystem or secret values.
          EOF
          cat > backup-output/README.txt <<'EOF'
          UFI complete source recovery archive.
          ufi-mirror.git contains all Git refs and commit history at backup time.
          branch-snapshots contains a source ZIP for every branch/tag ref listed in ALL_GIT_REFS.txt.
          RENDER_SERVICE_CONFIG.txt records known deployment settings; it does not contain secret values.

          cd backup-output

# Automatic data and exact-score validation: practical workaround

## What can and cannot be automatic

The static GitHub Pages UI cannot safely hold a paid-provider API key. Browser-only calls either expose the key or depend on public feeds that do not cover every league or metric. The reliable pattern is:

1. **Frontend:** GitHub Pages remains the existing UI.
2. **Secret-safe data proxy:** deploy `tools/ufi_live_data_proxy.py` to a small HTTPS backend (Replit, Render, or a VPS). Store `SPORTMONKS_API_TOKEN` as a server environment secret, never in JavaScript, HTML, Git, logs, or exported reports.
3. **Live match centre:** the proxy exposes `/api/live`, `/api/fixtures?date=YYYY-MM-DD`, `/api/fixture?id=PROVIDER_FIXTURE_ID`, and `/api/health`. The fixture response requests score/state, participants, events, lineups and statistics in one API call. The provider plan must cover the target league and xG fields.
4. **Refresh policy:** live scores/events are cached for 15 seconds; date fixtures for 5 minutes. Respect provider rate limits and plan quotas. If the provider fails, show stale/unknown data and its fetch time—never silently treat old data as current.
5. **Historical training:** continue using UFI's existing permitted OpenFootball and StatsBomb sources. Their coverage differs: results-only records can fit score-rate baselines; event/xG/player features require competitions with those fields. Do not fill missing xG with invented numbers.
6. **Forecast evaluation:** fit only on earlier matches and score on later unseen matches. Compare a league-average Poisson baseline, rolling team attack/defence rates, and the richer feature model on identical test fixtures. Log exact-score log loss, 1X2 Brier/log loss, calibration bins, coverage and sample size. Tactical/player features are retained only if the time-ordered test shows repeatable improvement.
7. **Live-state estimates:** after the score/time changes, update remaining-goal distributions using historical match states matched by minute, score difference, red cards and pre-match strength. Until that conditional model has adequate out-of-time validation, display current score/events and mark live probabilities **experimental / unavailable**, rather than pretending the pre-match Poisson grid is a live model.

## Provider choice and cost reality

Sportmonks documents a single fixture endpoint with nested score, state, event, lineup and statistics includes, plus xG where included by the plan. See [its match-centre integration guide](https://www.sportmonks.com/blogs/how-to-build-a-match-page-with-the-sportmonks-football-api/). The public/free plan is limited by league coverage, and paid coverage/quotas can change. Confirm the leagues and xG entitlement before paying. The proxy is provider-specific; it does not make a provider subscription free.

Alternative providers include API-Football; its documentation describes fixtures/events, lineups and player statistics, but the exact fields and access depend on the plan: [API-Football docs](https://apifootball.com/documentation/).

## Deploy the proxy

- Deploy `tools/ufi_live_data_proxy.py` as a Python web service with start command `python tools/ufi_live_data_proxy.py`.
- Add environment variable `SPORTMONKS_API_TOKEN` in the hosting provider's secret/environment settings.
- Set `UFI_ALLOWED_ORIGIN` to the exact deployed UFI Pages origin, e.g. `https://edoborrmagdalene9-dev.github.io` (or the origin actually shown in your address bar).
- Use HTTPS; do not expose an unauthenticated proxy to unrestricted public use. Add host-level access control/rate limits before public production use.
- Test `/api/health`, then `/api/fixtures?date=YYYY-MM-DD`, then `/api/live`. An empty response may mean no fixtures, unavailable league coverage, or provider schema/plan restrictions; inspect the provider's response safely without printing the token.
- The current static exact-score panel is still a manual-input baseline until the API endpoint is connected in the UI. This proxy creates the safe data route, but does not itself deploy the backend or activate provider access.

## Honest acceptance gates

- **Historical calibration:** use a chronological train/validation/test split; freeze each forecast before kickoff; compare against baselines on the same fixtures; report confidence intervals and sample size.
- **Live state:** verify minute, score, event sequence and data age; reconcile duplicate/out-of-order events; invalidate or flag stale feeds; train and validate remaining-goal probabilities on historical in-play snapshots.
- **Lineups/cards/substitutions/shots/xG:** store source name, provider match ID, source timestamp, fetched timestamp, field-level missingness and corrections. xG is provider/model-specific; do not mix it as if definitions are identical.
- **Tactical/player feature tests:** ablate feature groups one at a time on chronological unseen fixtures. Report log loss/Brier changes and uncertainty. Do not claim a feature helps merely because it explains one miss.
- **Fail-safe:** if API key, provider, coverage, freshness or data-quality checks fail, show unavailable/unknown and fall back only to clearly labelled historical baseline. No “sure score” or automatic stake escalation.

# 5400 Bookie-Edge Gate

## Purpose
The gate screens candidate markets for conservative expected value and data quality. It does not infer bookmaker intent from a suspended market or price movement, and it cannot guarantee a winning selection.

## 5400-second temporal margin rule
A standard 90-minute match contains 5,400 seconds. For elapsed time (t), capped to the regulation 90-minute frame, the requested heuristic is:

- `timeWeight = elapsedSeconds / 5400`
- `impliedProbability = 1 / decimalOdds`
- `marginScore = (modelProbability / impliedProbability) × (1 + timeWeight)`
- The temporal margin gate fails when `marginScore < 1.05`.

This score is displayed alongside the other metrics. **It is not a calibrated probability or proof that a model becomes more accurate as time passes.** The time multiplier can make a weak prediction appear stronger late in a match; therefore passing this score alone never approves a selection. Conservative EV, conservative edge, source freshness, uncertainty, and validation filters remain mandatory. A live implementation should use the live state-conditioned probability for the current score and remaining time, not the pre-match probability.

## 5400 observation/checkpoint rule
The interface maps elapsed match time to at most 5,400 elapsed seconds. It reevaluates when the user or live-data pipeline supplies fresh inputs; it does not manufacture 5,400 independent observations from repeated values, and it cannot certify future checkpoints that have not occurred. A selection only passes the current evaluation when all configured hard filters pass.

## Candidate JSON
Each candidate requires:
- `selection`
- `odds`: decimal odds > 1
- `modelProbability`: estimated probability from 0 to 1, strictly between endpoints
- `marketProbability`: de-vigged market probability from 0 to 1
- `uncertainty`: uncertainty estimate in probability units (0 to 1)
- `sourceUpdatedAt`: source-side ISO timestamp, not just browser capture time
- `validationMatches`: number of independent out-of-sample validation matches
- `snapshots`: distinct timestamped market snapshots

The conservative lower probability is `max(0, modelProbability - 1.645 × uncertainty)`. Conservative EV is `lowerProbability × decimalOdds - 1`. Conservative edge is `lowerProbability - marketProbability`. The selection is discarded when source data is stale/future-dated, fields are invalid, validation/snapshot counts are too small, uncertainty exceeds the limit, probability is below the floor, the temporal margin score is below 1.05, conservative EV is below the threshold, or conservative edge is too small.

This lower-bound calculation is a heuristic screening rule, not a statistically calibrated confidence interval unless the uncertainty input is calibrated accordingly. Configure marketProbability from a correctly de-vigged market for the same outcome/market; don't compare a score probability with a 1X2 probability.

## Limitations
- No provider API is assumed to be connected to this gate automatically.
- It is a screening UI and doesn't place bets.
- A bookmaker may suspend markets for many operational or risk reasons; suspension is not proof of a trap.
- Odds and probabilities are uncertain; even a candidate passing the gate can lose.
- Thresholds must be tuned and evaluated on chronological unseen data. Do not use the feature as proof of profit or as a guarantee.

# Crainee Background Engine — Match-Can-Break-This-Way Simulator

This additive module flags hypothetical structural fractures using an underdog head-start toggle, defensive-error tendency, transition pacing, weather friction, referee disruption and a simplified 5400-unit pitch coordinate.

## Important corrections to the supplied draft
- `underdogScoredFirst` is true only when explicitly supplied as true. The original `value || true` made it always true.
- Missing nested objects receive safe defaults rather than throwing.
- Inputs are range-checked.
- The existing confidence-percentage feature is retained as a heuristic signal-strength indicator: `confidence = min(100, ruptureIndex / breakThreshold × 100)`. `confidenceStatus` is `HEURISTIC_SIGNAL_PERCENT_NOT_CALIBRATED_PROBABILITY` and `confidenceIsProbability` is `false`. This percentage is **not** the chance of an upset; it only indicates how strongly this hand-built index crosses its configured threshold.
- Scorelines are scenario candidates for exploration, not model probabilities or guaranteed outcomes.

## Example
```js
const {simulateMatchBreakScenarios}=require('./crainee-background-break-engine');
const result=simulateMatchBreakScenarios({
  ballPositionUnit: 5400,
  underdogScoredFirst: true,
  favorite: {defenderErrorTendency: 8},
  underdog: {attackerTransitionPacing: 9},
  environment: {isHeavyRainOrWind: false},
  referee: {whistleFrequencyChaos: 1.4}
});
```

Both rupture index and confidence percentage are heuristic signals, not calibrated probabilities. For example, 62.5% does **not** mean a 62.5% chance of an upset. Validate and calibrate against held-out historical fixtures before interpreting any output probabilistically.

# Crainee Temporal, Squad & Referee Chaos Gate

This is an experimental contextual-risk heuristic that can be used alongside the 5400 Bookie-Edge Gate. It does not establish a positive betting edge and is not a calibrated probability model.

## Formula
For each player, the squad asset contribution is:

`marketValue × exp(-0.015 × (age - 27)^2) × log10(caps + 10)`

Missing player fields use the user's example defaults (market value 1,000,000; age 26; caps 30) and the UI reports them. Team contributions are summed. Squad power is `clamp(log10(homeScore + awayScore + 1) / 15, 0, 1)`.

The referee flow factor is `min(1.2, activeAverageWhistleGap / 100)`, selecting the first-half or second-half supplied gap by elapsed time. Time weight is `clamp(currentSecond, 0, 5400) / 5400`. Composite score is `squadPower × refereeFlowFactor × (1 + timeWeight)`. A score below 0.25 is discarded.

For an eligible score, the maximum stake cap is `min(5%, 5% × compositeScore)` of the entered bankroll. This is a mechanical cap, not advice to stake that amount; a zero bankroll yields zero. No forced minimum stake is used.

## Limitations and required validation
- Market value, age and caps do not by themselves model availability, playing time, position, form, tactics, or causal match outcomes.
- Average time between whistles is a weak proxy for referee style; historical distributions and match-specific events are preferable.
- The time multiplier increases the composite score with elapsed time by construction. It does not prove growing forecast accuracy.
- Do not treat a high score as an expected-value edge. Combine only with independently calibrated outcome probabilities and same-market, de-vigged prices.
- The interface uses user-supplied squad and referee data; it is not connected to a live provider automatically.
- The gate cannot guarantee profit. Test chronologically on unseen matches and compare with baselines before relying on it.

# Crainee Subconscious Neural Databank & Fan Narrative Engine

This is an additive module; it does not replace existing engines.

Pipeline:
1. Positional sampler evaluates units 0–5400 every 180 units (31 samples), comparing identical height, composure, foot-skill and style-fit inputs across home/away DEFENSE, MIDFIELD and ATTACK.
2. Reality Supervisor applies the requested favorite (2:1) or underdog (1:1) baseline vector, score caps and scoreline/zone deduplication.
3. Fan Narrative Matcher adds conditional narrative hypotheses from missing-player names, weather and referee-disruption inputs and ranks candidates with the existing friction/context heuristic.
4. Full pipeline returns raw candidates, filtered candidates, ranked candidates, a short list and caveats.

Browser global: window.CraineeSubconsciousNarrativeEngine
Node module: require('./crainee-subconscious-narrative-engine.js')

Limitations: the grid is a simplified sample, not tracking data; the loop is not a microsecond simulator; no neural network, fan-sentiment corpus or automatic live feed is fabricated. Fan narrative is descriptive context, not predictive evidence. Quality scores are heuristic rankings, not probabilities. Validate chronologically held-out fixtures against baselines before making predictive claims.

 
## Subconscious pattern bank and fan-reaction ranking (v2)
- Fan-reaction candidates are generated per candidate scoreline for both fan bases, including tight-game/missed-chance draw reactions, dominance, referee complaints, defensive errors, finishing, missing starters, weather, streaks, coach pressure and caller-supplied narratives.
- The best matching reaction materially contributes to the ranking score. This is a deliberate candidate-ranking heuristic, not a calibrated probability.
- `calculateCrossDomainSignals` accepts numeric sequences from other domains and extracts momentum, normalized volatility, mean-reversion distance and a generic pattern signal. These features can be supplied as extra context, but their relevance to football is unproven until tested on chronological holdout matches; unrelated numbers are not magically predictive.
- The UI exposes a 5,400-unit clock advancing 180 units every 3 seconds. One sweep takes 90 wall-clock seconds; the display is a simulation clock, not live match time or actual microsecond computation.
- Missing players, weather, referee disruption, defensive-error signal, tactical-shift signal, team names/streaks and optional custom fan narratives are supported as caller-provided context. This does not automatically fetch those facts.

# Crainee Tactical Surprise Engine

Additive browser/CommonJS module: `crainee-tactical-surprise-engine.js`. It exposes three component evaluators and a combined evaluator, and injects a new Tactical Surprise page into the existing UFI UI without rewriting existing modules.

## Components
- **Spatial grid:** one-dimensional 0–5400 ball coordinate, zone-based rating mismatch and spatial stretch.
- **Tactical edge:** defensive-line height × transition speed, winger/fullback mismatch, time progression and fatigue/substitute inputs.
- **Style friction:** explicitly labelled player-style matchup signals and a nonlinear interaction with grid position.
- **Combined report:** shows each subscore, flags and limitations; it does not convert them to probabilities.

## Important corrections versus the supplied draft
- Values are validated and bounded; zero is not silently replaced by JavaScript's `||` defaults.
- Original spatial midpoint and zone logic are retained in spirit, but the style engine uses a consistent exported namespace and handles missing teams safely.
- The supplied draft's “confidence” percentages and “Bookie margin bypassed” claim were removed. These are heuristic scores without calibration evidence, not exploit probabilities or a guaranteed shock prediction.
- Time/fatigue growth is restrained, and all output is explicitly labelled as an experimental signal.

## Inputs and data limitations
The UI uses user-entered ratings and example JSON; it does not connect a live player-tracking, lineup, fatigue, or event provider. 0–5400 is a conceptual coordinate, not a physical pitch model or player tracking stream. Defaults are illustrative and must be replaced by sourced observations.

## Validation
Run `node --test tests/crainee-tactical-surprise-engine.test.cjs`. Tests cover all three components, invalid ranges, asymmetric styles, and combined output. This does not establish predictive lift. Before treating the engine as predictive, evaluate chronologically on historical matches using only pre-match information for pre-match forecasts, compare with existing baselines, and report calibration and exact-score log loss/Brier score where applicable.

# Crainee Unconventional Outcome Engine

An additive exact-score exploration module for Universal Football Intelligence. It deliberately generates a separate shortlist that is not filtered by bookmaker odds or the main model's ranking.

## Behaviour
- **Break away from the favourite:** starts from a broad bounded score grid, excludes the main model's exact pick, and ranks materially different candidates for exploration.
- **Alternate realities:** includes early underdog goal, red-card disruption, favourite dominates but blanks, clinical underdog, chaotic open game, and late equaliser paths.
- **Controlled randomness:** pass a seed to reproduce the same candidate list. The result includes the seed and a stable seed hash.
- **Protect unexpected candidates:** returns `unconventionalShortlist` separately from optional `conventionalShortlist`. Each unconventional candidate is labelled `EXPLORED_POSSIBILITY`, has `probability: null`, and is never represented as a calibrated probability.

## API
```js
const { generateUnconventionalOutcomes } = require('./crainee-unconventional-outcome-engine');
const result = generateUnconventionalOutcomes({
  seed: 'fixture-2026-10-25-experiment-01',
  homeTeam: 'Home FC',
  awayTeam: 'Away FC',
  mainModelScore: { home: 2, away: 1 },
  conventionalShortlist: [{ home: 2, away: 1 }, { home: 1, away: 1 }],
  candidateCount: 12,
  maxGoals: 5
});
```

Candidates are exploratory possibilities, not probability estimates or betting recommendations. Randomness increases scenario coverage but does not itself make a forecast more accurate. Improvement must be measured on chronological, untouched fixtures against the existing model and simple baselines.

# Exact-Score Intelligence: Post-Mortems, Robust Scoreline Forecasts, and Risk Controls

## Purpose

This guide extends Universal Football Intelligence (UFI) without removing or replacing existing modules. It is a research and decision-support specification, not a promise that exact-score picks can be made safe. Exact-score betting is intrinsically high variance; the app must prefer calibrated probabilities, honest uncertainty, and post-match learning over confident-sounding tips.

## Case file: 23 October 2026

### Recorded selections and outcomes

- Stuttgart vs Borussia Mönchengladbach: selected 2–1 at decimal odds 9.40; reported full-time result 2–3. Exact-score miss.
- Bolton Wanderers vs Middlesbrough: selected 1–1 at decimal odds 9.00; the supplied in-play screenshot showed 1–3 at 66 minutes. This was already a severe state mismatch for a 1–1 forecast. Do not record a final result unless independently verified.

### Case file: 25 October 2026 screen recording

The user-provided SportyBet ticket recording visibly showed:
- **Inter vs Fiorentina:** selected 2–1 at 8.30; displayed full-time result 2–1. This exact-score selection won.
- **Athletic Bilbao vs Getafe:** selected 1–0 at 5.60; displayed full-time result 4–0. This exact-score selection lost.
- **Freiburg vs Bayern Munich:** Over 1.5 at 1.35; displayed full-time result 1–3, so that totals selection won.
- **Barcelona vs Real Madrid:** Over 1.5 at 1.29 was still pending in the visible recording; no outcome is inferred here.

The visible multi-ticket itself was settled as lost. This is an outcome audit of the shown ticket, not proof that every listed selection was generated by the model.

### What the Bilbao–Getafe miss should teach UFI

A 1–0 point pick did not cover the observed 4–0 outcome. The next version should score the **whole probability distribution**, including high-score tails, and test whether it systematically underestimates a strong favourite's multi-goal wins or over-concentrates on narrow scores. Add a post-match flag for a large scoreline residual (for example, the observed total goals or goal difference falling far outside the forecast's high-probability region), then inspect pre-kickoff strength, team goal rates, lineup/keeper availability, red cards, penalties, event timeline, shots/xG and game-state changes where verified. Do not assign a cause from the final score alone.

The Inter–Fiorentina hit is useful positive evidence for the ledger, but one correct score does not validate the model. Evaluate both hits and misses on the same unseen fixtures with log loss, calibration and baseline comparisons. The fact that the 4–0 happened does not establish that the bookmaker deliberately opposed the selection; a football result alone cannot prove intent.

## What this case proves—and does not prove

It proves that the selected exact scores failed to match the known match states. It does **not** prove one specific tactical or player-level cause. A defensible post-mortem needs verified event timelines, starting lineups, substitutions, cards, shots and shots on target, xG if available, game state, and pre-kickoff odds snapshots. Never invent a reason for a goal or claim that a model could have known it without evidence.

The two selections also illustrate a process failure: a single most-likely-looking score can be mistaken for a strong prediction. Exact-score probability is usually spread across many outcomes. Odds of 9.40 imply a raw break-even probability of about 10.64%; odds of 9.00 imply about 11.11%, before accounting for bookmaker margin and market conditions. Those prices are not evidence that the score is likely.

## Required UFI workflow

### 1. Freeze a pre-match forecast

For every fixture, persist an immutable timestamped record containing:

- fixture ID, competition, kickoff time and timezone;
- data source, retrieval time, freshness, missing fields and source reliability;
- expected goals for each team (lambda), method and uncertainty interval;
- a complete scoreline probability grid (at least 0–0 through 5–5, with a tail bucket);
- 1X2, totals and both-teams-to-score probabilities derived from the same distribution;
- market odds timestamp, market type, bookmaker, implied probability and margin-removal method;
- lineup/injury/suspension confidence and any unconfirmed assumptions;
- model version, feature version and forecast creation time.

Do not overwrite the pre-match forecast with post-match knowledge. Store live updates as separate forecast versions.

### 2. Generate a distribution, not just one score

Use the existing Poisson score grid as a baseline, with Dixon–Coles low-score dependence only when implemented and tested. Compare against a simple league-frequency baseline and an independent goal-rate baseline. Do not treat more complex models as better until they win on chronological out-of-sample tests.

Return:
- the top scorelines and their individual probabilities;
- cumulative probability of the displayed shortlist;
- the probability mass outside the displayed grid;
- uncertainty or data-quality warnings;
- the scoreline range that covers a chosen cumulative probability, rather than calling one score “sure.”

A shortlist of different scores is for scenario coverage, not a guarantee. If the user chooses multiple scores, report overlap, combined coverage under the model, cost, and the probability of all selections losing.

### 3. Live-state override

If the match has started, never reuse a pre-match scoreline as if nothing changed. Recalculate conditional probabilities from the current minute and score using a live model that has been separately validated. Incorporate, when verified and available:

- current score and elapsed/added time;
- red cards and player dismissals;
- substitutions and lineup changes;
- shots, shots on target, xG, big chances and set pieces;
- attacking pressure and possession only with context (raw possession alone is not a goal forecast);
- injuries, stoppages and time remaining;
- pre-match strength and current game state.

If no validated live model or reliable live feed is available, mark the live probability as unavailable; do not fabricate it. Suspended odds are not a reliable signal by themselves.

### 4. Explain scoreline failures using evidence

After the match, obtain verified final score and event timeline. Build a post-match audit:

1. Did the actual result fall inside the model's high-probability region?
2. Was the forecast wrong because the goal rates were misestimated, because of red cards/penalties/early goals, or because the scoreline distribution was miscalibrated?
3. Did late game state produce open play, defensive collapse, time-wasting, or a comeback? Record only if supported by match events or credible reporting.
4. Were lineups, injuries, schedule congestion, travel, weather, tactical matchups, or referee tendencies actually known pre-match?
5. Was any key input missing, stale, incorrectly mapped, or sourced after kickoff?
6. Did the model overvalue recent form, head-to-head results, a favorite label, or one exact score?
7. What would the baseline have forecast, and did the advanced model improve on it?

Use a structured reason taxonomy: data_error, lineup_uncertainty, goal_rate_error, scoreline_dependence, red_card, penalty, early_goal_game_state, late_game_state, finishing_variance, keeper_variance, set_piece, model_calibration, market_price, unknown. A reason can be unknown; never force a causal explanation.

### 5. Historical validation gates

Evaluate chronologically, never with random train/test splitting across time. Each forecast must use only information available before kickoff. Report:

- exact-score log loss / ranked probability score where appropriate;
- multiclass Brier score and calibration plots;
- 1X2 log loss and Brier score;
- top-1 exact-score hit rate with sample size and confidence interval;
- top-k scoreline coverage with sample size;
- comparison against league-frequency, independent Poisson, and market-implied baselines;
- results by league, season, odds band and data-quality tier;
- performance on untouched test dates;
- profit/ROI only when complete timestamped, pre-kickoff odds and settlement rules are available, with commission/margin and drawdown included.

A small sample of correct or incorrect bets is not proof of skill. Do not tune the model on the same matches used to report final performance.

### 6. Odds and selection discipline

For decimal odds O, raw implied probability is 1/O. Remove market margin only using a documented method and a complete, same-market odds set. Compare model and market probabilities on the same outcome definition. Flag an apparent edge only if it survives probability uncertainty and data-quality checks.

Do not rank selections by payout alone. Do not automatically prefer long odds, favorites, or contrarian scores. “Wicked” should mean rigorous, skeptical, and willing to reject a tempting pick—not reckless. If no demonstrable edge exists, the correct output is **NO BET / PAPER TRACK ONLY**.

Never increase stakes to recover a previous loss. Avoid stake escalation, chasing, and claims of guaranteed or “sure” scores. The app must clearly separate research forecasts from betting recommendations and include a reminder that all exact-score outcomes can lose.

## App implementation checklist

- [ ] Add a new Exact-Score Post-Mortem research guide linked from the research/help area.
- [ ] Add a structured forecast snapshot schema and keep pre-match/live/post-match records separate.
- [ ] Add a scoreline-grid view with probabilities, cumulative shortlist coverage, and tail mass.
- [ ] Add a match-event audit form with source links and an explicit unknown reason.
- [ ] Add a data-freshness and lineup-confidence gate before a forecast can be marked usable.
- [ ] Add a chronological evaluation report with baseline comparisons and calibration metrics.
- [ ] Add a hard warning when an exact score is displayed without calibrated out-of-sample evidence.
- [ ] Add a live-score mismatch warning; never present a stale pre-match pick as a current live forecast.
- [ ] Preserve all existing features and routes; this is an additive module.
- [ ] Add tests for probability normalization, timestamp leakage, missing data, score-state updates, market-margin handling, and no-bet decisions.
- [ ] Run the existing test suite and report the actual result. Do not claim implementation or tests are complete until verified.

## Acceptance criteria

The module is not considered validated until it can replay historical fixtures in date order, reproduce each forecast from data available at that time, show calibration and baseline comparisons on held-out dates, and explain missing-data limitations. A guide file alone does not mean the app's model has changed or that the model has been tested.

# Live market shock, weather lookup, and unseen-match validation

## Added app capabilities

- **Live match feed:** existing UFI proxy now requests score/state, events, lineups, statistics, referees and venue where the provider supplies them. The browser refresh interval is 10 seconds while the match feed is connected; the proxy cache is 5 seconds for live/fixture detail. This is polling, not a millisecond data stream.
- **Weather lookup:** `/api/weather?lat=..&lon=..&date=YYYY-MM-DD&hour=0..23` uses Open-Meteo hourly temperature, relative humidity, precipitation and wind. No API key is required. The app requires the user to supply verified stadium coordinates and kickoff hour in UTC. It can call Open-Meteo directly from the browser without a backend or key, or use the proxy weather route if a backend is configured. It returns source/fetch metadata and warns that gridded weather is not a stadium sensor.
- **Automatic in-play odds adapter:** `/api/inplay-odds?id=FIXTURE_ID&bookmaker=BOOKMAKER_ID&market=MARKET_ID` fetches a single fixture/bookmaker/market from Sportmonks when the account plan includes in-play odds. The browser can poll every 5 seconds and records both capture time and the provider's last-update timestamp. Match-winner labels must include Home, Draw and Away; incomplete market data is rejected. Free plans may not include in-play odds, and a provider bookmaker ID is not the same thing as SportyBet.
- **Market shock circuit breaker:** the app compares two timestamped 1X2 decimal-odds snapshots from the same market/bookmaker, removes each snapshot's overround by normalizing implied probabilities, calculates probability changes and relative price movement, and checks timestamp freshness. A large shift or stale/future timestamp pauses the current forecast. A modest fresh move only makes a small bounded adjustment to user-supplied remaining-goal xG; it does not infer a goal, red card, penalty or cause from prices alone.
- **Chronological exact-score backtest:** upload CSV columns `date,home_team,away_team,home_goals,away_goals` (optional `league` or `league_code`). Forecasts use earlier matches only; all matches on the same date are predicted before that date is added to history. It compares a rolling team attack/defence Poisson model against a league-average Poisson baseline, reporting out-of-sample exact-score top-1 hit rate and exact-score negative log likelihood. Results can be exported as CSV.

## Why the model must be allowed to discard a match

A sharp price move is a **review trigger**, not proof that the bookmaker is hiding a result or that a goal is certain. Possible causes include an actual event, a feed correction, a suspension/reopen, liquidity, differing bookmaker prices, or market noise. UFI should discard or pause when:
- snapshot timestamps are missing, stale, out of order or in the future;
- prices are not from the same market/bookmaker/market definition;
- the score/minute does not reconcile with the event feed;
- live feed age or provider status exceeds the configured freshness limit;
- the model's score-state inputs are incomplete or inconsistent.

Do not use an odds shock alone to force a scoreline or raise stakes. After a shock, refresh the match events and verify score, minute, cards, penalties, substitutions and VAR status. If the cause remains unknown, keep the forecast paused.

## Data-source limits

Sportmonks is the configured football provider in the backend adapter; the account must have a valid server-side `SPORTMONKS_API_TOKEN` and coverage for the competition/fields. The code cannot create a provider account, grant a subscription, or deploy a backend without the user's hosting/account authorization. Sportmonks documentation notes that in-play odds require advanced data access on supported plans; if the endpoint is unavailable, the monitor stops instead of inventing odds. The static GitHub Pages app cannot securely store provider secrets. Open-Meteo is the weather source and requires location/time inputs, but its public no-key API can be called directly from the browser.

Official references:
- Sportmonks API documentation: https://docs.sportmonks.com/football/
- Sportmonks football API and coverage: https://www.sportmonks.com/football-api/
- Sportmonks in-play odds documentation: https://docs.sportmonks.com/v3/tutorials-and-guides/tutorials/odds-and-predictions/live-inplay-odds
- Open-Meteo API documentation: https://open-meteo.com/en/docs
- Football-Data.org API reference (alternative basic fixtures/results/lineups coverage): https://www.football-data.org/documentation/api

## Validation scope and acceptance gate

The backtest is a results-only walk-forward diagnostic. It does **not** yet backtest all live event, referee, lineup, weather, player-foot, odds-shock and tactical inputs, because those require timestamped historical snapshots aligned to each match. Do not claim those features improve accuracy until each feature group is tested on the same unseen fixtures and beats simple baselines on proper scoring rules.

Before calling this production-ready:
1. Deploy the proxy to a controlled HTTPS host and configure the token as a server secret.
2. Confirm provider competition coverage and that returned fixture payloads actually include referees, venue, lineups, events and stats for the selected league.
3. Verify source timestamps and stale-feed behaviour during live matches.
4. Run historical CSV tests with 100+ matches per evaluated competition where possible; preserve a final date range that is not used for tuning.
5. Add archived odds snapshots and live-state snapshots if odds-shock and in-play probabilities are to be calibrated retrospectively.
6. Report sample sizes, log loss, Brier score/calibration, baseline comparison and confidence intervals. A high top-1 exact-score hit rate on a tiny sample is not proof of an edge.

# UFI player intelligence registry

This CSV is an evidence ledger, not an automatic player-truth database. Start with `data/private/player_registry.csv` copied from `data/templates/player_registry_template.csv`. Do not fill unknown values by guessing.

## Fields and evidence rules

- `player_id`: stable ID from the cited source when available; never merge names alone if identity is uncertain.
- `source_name` and `source_url`: provenance for each claim. Keep source terms/licensing in mind.
- `preferred_foot`: use `left`, `right`, `both` or `unknown`.
- `weak_foot_evidence`: specific observable evidence and sample context; do not infer weak foot from one highlight.
- `role` and `observed_style_tags`: analyst labels supported by match evidence, not personality claims.
- `efootball_style_tags`: separate game-specific labels such as Fox in the Box or Orchestrator. These are not official real-world labels.
- `minutes_observed`, starts, substitute appearances and evidence window: exposure context matters.
- `confidence_0_to_1`: confidence in the recorded claim, with a note explaining why.
- `record_type`: `source_fact`, `analyst_observation` or `model_estimate`.
- `analyst_notes`: include counterexamples and uncertainty, not only supporting evidence.

## Substitution impact

For each substitution, preserve match ID, minute, score at entry, red-card state if known, incoming/outgoing player IDs, role, source and event evidence. Compare similar match states and minutes; raw win/loss after entry is not causal proof of impact.

## Current data availability

OpenFootball has no player registry fields, lineups, footedness or tracking. The template intentionally contains headers only. Populate it from sources you are authorized to use; the model must not invent missing facts.


## Event-based player profile (browser app)

The Player Intelligence module can now build a descriptive profile for each player who has an attributed event in the currently loaded StatsBomb match. It summarizes event count, shots, goals, source-provided xG, passes and completed-pass event convention, carries, pressures, duels, interceptions, recoveries, clearances, blocks, assists/key-pass indicators where supplied, and first/last recorded event minute. When lineups have separately been loaded, it also marks players found in the lineup and records source position labels. The table can be exported to CSV.

This is deliberately a **single-match event summary**, not a player ability score. Event coverage varies by competition/season; first/last event minutes are not minutes played; no event summary establishes preferred foot, weak foot, substitute impact, or a universal style such as “Orchestrator”. Those require separate source-backed evidence and multi-match exposure. This feature does not yet feed the prediction model.


## Multi-match player evidence dataset

The browser app can save the current single-match event profile into a local player-match evidence dataset. Repeat for additional matches; rows are deduplicated by match ID, team and player. The aggregate table sums observed event counts across saved matches and shows match count and date window. It does **not** calculate per-90 rates because reliable player minutes are not established by first/last event timestamps. It does not yet feed forecasts. Export the CSV or workspace backup to move the data to another device; local browser storage does not sync automatically. Identity changes, transfers, competition coverage and source omissions must be reviewed before comparing players.


## Approximate appearance and minutes evidence (new)

When a loaded StatsBomb lineup explicitly labels a player as part of the Starting XI, the app records a starter status. When a substitution event names a replacement, the app records an estimated exposure window from the substitution minute to the last recorded event minute; a starter's window ends at their recorded substitution minute or the last event minute. These are **rough clock-minute estimates**, not official minutes played: stoppage time, period-clock details, red cards, missing lineups and source-specific event coverage can introduce errors. The app labels the method and confidence, includes the fields in CSV export, and must not use these values for per-90 rates or forecasts until validated against an authoritative minutes source. A lineup-listed player without an evidenced start or substitution is not assumed to have appeared.

# UFI integrated Phase 1–3 pipeline

The integrated runner coordinates match prediction, player-evidence auditing, and timestamp-aware paper validation in one run.

## Run locally

From the repository root with Python 3.10+ (standard library only):

```bash
python -m unittest discover -s tests -p "test_*.py" -v
python tools/ufi_integrated_pipeline.py --matches output/openfootball/matches.csv --out output/openfootball/integrated
```

The matches CSV can be created with the OpenFootball downloader documented in [openfootball.md](data-sources/openfootball.md). The integrated runner calls the advanced chronological backtest and writes:

- `output/openfootball/integrated/advanced/metrics.json`
- `output/openfootball/integrated/advanced/advanced_predictions.csv`
- `output/openfootball/integrated/advanced/paper_betting_ledger.csv`
- `output/openfootball/integrated/ufi_integrated_report.json`
- `output/openfootball/integrated/ufi_integrated_report.md`

## Phase 1 — match model

The current benchmark compares Elo, rolling venue-specific goal-rate Poisson, a fixed 50/50 ensemble, and a league-frequency baseline. All fixtures on a calendar date are forecast before results from that date update the model because this results source does not provide reliable kickoff timestamps. Review accuracy, Brier score and log loss together; lower Brier/log loss are better.

## Phase 2 — player evidence

Copy `data/templates/player_registry_template.csv` to the git-ignored path `data/private/player_registry.csv` and populate only sourced observations. Keep unknown footedness/style values unknown, cite source URLs, and separate real-world observations from game-specific tags such as “Fox in the Box” and “Orchestrator”.

The integrated pipeline audits player records and evidence coverage. It deliberately does **not** use player fields in match forecasts yet: the OpenFootball results-only dataset does not identify which players appeared in each match, their minutes, or the timestamp at which player information became available. The next integration requirement is a licensed/suitable player-match participation dataset with lineups, starts/sub appearances and minutes, joined to fixtures using stable player IDs and kickoff timing. Until then, player-aware predictions would be unsupported.

## Phase 3 — odds and paper validation

Copy `data/templates/pre_match_odds_template.csv` to the git-ignored path `data/private/pre_match_odds.csv` and add odds only when the source terms permit your intended use. Required columns include:

- Match key: `date,league_code,home_team,away_team`
- Decimal odds: `home_odds,draw_odds,away_odds`
- Time evidence: `captured_at_utc,kickoff_at_utc` as timezone-aware ISO 8601 timestamps, with capture time strictly earlier than kickoff
- Provenance: `source_name,source_url`

Run the integrated command again. Only matched records with valid odds and pre-kickoff timestamps enter the flat-one-unit paper ledger. ROI remains null when no eligible odds join exists. Never put private data, API keys or credentials in this public repository; `data/private/` is git-ignored.

## Current gate status and honest limits

A successful workflow means the pipeline executed and its checks passed. It does not mean all three data requirements are complete: the default public workflow has results but no sourced player records or supplied odds, so player-aware forecasting remains gated and betting ROI is not computed. No model can guarantee winning bets. Evaluate on multiple unseen chronological windows and compare to a market baseline before making claims about edge.


## Probability-versus-market audit (new)

After generating historical predictions, compare them with odds that were captured before kickoff and that your source permits you to use. The audit removes the raw 1X2 overround by normalizing implied probabilities, records the model-minus-market probability gap for Home/Draw/Away, and scores both probability vectors with Brier score and log loss.

```bash
python tools/ufi_probability_market_audit.py \
  --predictions output/openfootball/integrated/advanced/advanced_predictions.csv \
  --odds-csv data/private/pre_match_odds.csv \
  --out output/openfootball/integrated/market_audit
```

Outputs:
- `probability_market_audit.csv` — one row per matched historical fixture, with timestamp evidence, normalized market probabilities, probability gaps and forecast scores.
- `probability_market_audit.json` — aggregate comparison and counts of unmatched/rejected records.

The tool rejects missing/timezone-free timestamps, odds captured at or after kickoff, invalid decimal odds, and invalid model probability vectors. Duplicate fixture keys are excluded as ambiguous. Negative mean model-minus-market score differences mean the model had lower average loss on that matched historical sample; they do not prove a future edge or profitability. The tool does not recommend selections. Run it across multiple chronological periods and retain all eligible rows, including poor forecasts.

# UFI next-phase roadmap: from results to football intelligence

This roadmap keeps UFI grounded in observable football evidence. It does not treat missing player facts as known, and it separates match-result forecasting from player/tactical research.

## Phase 1 — trustworthy historical foundation

- Download permitted public-domain fixtures/results through the OpenFootball workflow.
- Validate required columns, scored-match coverage, manifest row counts, and source provenance.
- Run regression tests before data ingestion.
- Forecast all fixtures on the same calendar date before adding any results from that date to training history.
- Compare the team-goal model with a simple rolling league-outcome baseline.
- Report accuracy, multiclass Brier score, log loss, sample count, skipped rows, and per-league metrics.
- Keep each prediction row so errors can be investigated, not just hidden in a single score.

## Phase 2 — richer match and team features

Add features only when their source and timestamp are known:
- rolling goals for/against, home/away splits, opponent strength, and rest days;
- recent form with decay, while avoiding double-counting overlapping windows;
- red cards and match events only from sources that provide them;
- odds snapshots only from a source whose terms permit this use, with capture timestamps;
- lineup and injury availability only where the source provides reliable records.

Every feature must be computed as it would have been known before kickoff. Missing information remains missing; it must not be silently filled with a guessed value.

## Phase 3 — player intelligence registry

Maintain one player record per stable source ID where available, with:
- player name, team, competition, season, and source identifiers;
- preferred foot, weak-foot evidence, role, and observed play-style labels;
- minutes, starts/substitute appearances, and the match window supporting each observation;
- source URL, observation date, confidence, and evidence notes;
- a distinction between directly sourced facts, analyst observations, and model-derived estimates.

eFootball labels such as “Fox in the Box” or “Orchestrator” are game-specific classifications. Store them as a separate tag set rather than asserting that a real player objectively has the same official label. A dominant foot or style should not be inferred from one clip or a small sample.

## Phase 4 — substitutes and tactical context

For every substitution, record minute, score at the time, outgoing/incoming player, role, and available event evidence. Evaluate impact against comparable match states and minutes, controlling for scoreline, opponent, red cards, and selection bias. A player entering when the team is already losing should not automatically be blamed for the result.

## Phase 5 — evaluation before betting use

- Keep chronological holdouts by season; never tune on the final test period.
- Compare probabilities against simple baselines and, when lawfully available, timestamped bookmaker odds.
- Evaluate calibration and log loss/Brier score, not accuracy alone.
- Report sample sizes and uncertainty; break results down by league and season.
- Track closing-line comparisons or returns only when odds and settlement rules are complete and independently verified.
- Never call a model profitable from historical accuracy alone; paper-test new versions before risking money.

## Current limits

OpenFootball results do not provide player footedness, lineups, injuries, bookmaker odds, event sequences, or continuous player tracking. Those require separate data sources and permissions. The current Poisson baseline is a first benchmark, not a complete football simulator or an automated sure-bet engine.
