# UFI research source register

This register links the supplied research list to the project's implementation decisions. It is a curated reading map, not a claim that every source has been fully read or independently peer-reviewed. Primary academic work and official dataset/operator documentation take precedence over secondary explainers and promotional blogs.

## A. Football aerodynamics and mechanics
1. John W. M. Bush, [The Aerodynamics of the Beautiful Game (2013)](https://thales.mit.edu/bush/wp-content/uploads/2013/11/Beautiful-Game-2013.pdf) — MIT mathematics author manuscript on drag, spin/Magnus effects, surface roughness and trajectories. Relevant to ball-flight physics, not direct match-result prediction. Related institutional record: [MIT DSpace](https://dspace.mit.edu/entities/publication/2e973612-e01f-4857-a455-983b1150f82b).
2. A. M. Nathan, [On the Flight of the American Football](https://arxiv.org/html/0706.0366v1) — comparative projectile mechanics; American football differs from soccer, so parameters cannot be transferred uncritically.
3. [The Magnus Effect and the American Football](https://www.usna.edu/MNEDept/_files/documents/brownell_files/JSE%2016.pdf) — comparative sports-ball aerodynamics.
4. Goff et al., [An Aerodynamic Analysis of Recent FIFA World Cup Balls](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1710.02784) — ball design/aerodynamics; needs appropriate measured conditions.
5. Asai et al., [The Aerodynamics of Soccer Balls](https://arxiv.org/pdf/nlin/0611033) — soccer-specific aerodynamics.
6. Flow Thermo Lab, [Magnus Effect on a Rotating Soccer Ball](https://www.flowthermolab.com/magnus-effect-on-a-rotating-soccer-ball/) — secondary explainer; verify against primary work.
7. ResearchGate, [Magnus effect role in sports especially football](https://www.researchgate.net/publication/383192218_An_exploration_of_Magnus_effect_role_in_sports_especially_in_football) — repository copy; verify publication metadata/methods.
8. Related work: Paudel & Yan, [High-fidelity full-flight soccer ball aerodynamics (published 2025)](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s00466-025-02705-2); Alam et al., [Effects of surface structure on soccer ball aerodynamics](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S1877705812016396); [MIT News: How does a soccer ball swerve?](https://news.mit.edu/2014/explained-how-does-a-soccer-ball-swerve-0617).

**Implementation limit:** the current app lacks measured spin, launch velocity, continuous ball trajectory, wind, humidity, turf friction and full tracking coordinates. These papers inform future physics experiments; do not fabricate those inputs from final scores or event locations.

## B. Statistical prediction
9. Dixon & Coles (1997), [Modelling Association Football Scores and Inefficiencies in the Football Betting Market](https://doi.org/10.1111/1467-9876.00065) — primary reference for score dependence and market analysis.
10. Dashee87, [Dixon-Coles and time weighting](https://dashee87.github.io/football/python/predicting-football-results-with-statistical-modelling-dixon-coles-and-time-weighting/) — educational implementation; audit assumptions and leakage controls.
11. GoalIQ, [Dixon-Coles explainer](https://www.goaliqai.com/dixon-coles-model-football-predictions/) — secondary explainer, not evidence of profit.
12. Football Bet Prediction, [Dixon-Coles model explanation](https://football-bet-prediction.com/articles/dixoncoles-model-explained-improving-poisson/) — secondary explainer; cross-check formulas.
13. ResearchGate, [An Exploration of Predictive Football Modelling](https://www.researchgate.net/publication/338028907_An_exploration_of_predictive_football_modelling) — inspect full text, sample, baselines and leakage handling.
14. Prediction Engine, [Dixon-Coles implementation guide](https://predictionengine.app/learn/dixon-coles-soccer-model) — secondary learning source.
15. Opisthokonta, [Statistical soccer scoring models and identifiability constraints](https://opisthokonta.net/?p=939) — practical team parameter discussion.
16. Karlis et al., [Extending Dixon-Coles to women's football](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2307.02139) — research extension; population transfer requires testing.

**Implementation rule:** compare Elo, Poisson and fitted Dixon-Coles chronologically; report Brier/log loss/calibration and compare with no-vig market probabilities. Complexity is not evidence of superiority.

## C. Spatial analytics and pitch control
17. Haziq J., [Pitch-control research proposal](https://haziqj.ml/teaching/fyp/research_proposals/18b9001.pdf) — proposal/learning resource; verify cited methods.
18. Tony ElHabr, [Soccer pitch control in R](https://tonyelhabr.rbind.io/posts/soccer-pitch-control-r/) — secondary implementation.
19. William Spearman, [Quantifying Pitch Control](https://www.researchgate.net/publication/334849056_Quantifying_Pitch_Control) — requires spatiotemporal player tracking.
20. [Scientific Reports pitch-control and stamina research (2025)](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41598-025-22778-6) — position, speed, acceleration and stamina as inputs.

**Implementation rule:** StatsBomb event coordinates are event locations, not continuous 25 Hz player tracking. Pitch control remains unavailable without legitimate tracking data.

## D. Betting prices, bankroll risk and promotions
21. Bettor Results, [Kelly Criterion guide](https://www.bettorresults.com/blog/kelly-criterion-bet-sizing-guide) — secondary risk-management explainer; sizing is highly sensitive to probability error.
22. SmartMatchPick, [Kelly Criterion in football betting](https://smartmatchpick.com/guides/kelly-criterion-football) — secondary explainer; verify assumptions.
23. BetCompare, [Bet9ja multi-bonus review](https://betcompare.ng/sports-betting-insights/bet9ja-multi-bonus-accumulator-bonus-review) — secondary, time-sensitive promotion information.
24. BetCompare, [SportyBet multiple-bet bonus terms](https://betcompare.ng/sports-betting-insights/sportybet-multiple-bet-bonus-full-terms-how-it-works-worked-example-2026) — secondary, time-sensitive.
25. Bet9ja Promotion Code portal, [Bonus terms](https://bet9japromotioncode.ng/bet9ja-bonus-terms) — third-party information; verify current operator terms.
26. SportyBet Nigeria, [Official bonuses and gifts help](https://www.sportybet.com/ng/m/help/how-to-play/others/bonuses-and-gifts) — operator source for its published terms.
27. SportyBet Tanzania, [Bonuses and gifts help](https://www.sportybet.com/tz/m/help/how-to-play/others/bonuses-and-gifts) — different jurisdiction; do not assume Nigerian terms match.
28. Bet9ja Shop, [Retail shop terms](https://shop.bet9ja.com/pages/sportfooter_terms/content) — check version and applicability.

**Implementation rule:** raw implied probability is 1/decimal odds; normalize complete markets for an initial no-vig comparison. A bonus changes payout terms, not match probability. Accumulator dependence and settlement rules matter.

## E. Dataset documentation and related primary sources
- [StatsBomb Open Data event specification](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/statsbomb/open-data/master/doc/StatsBomb%20Open%20Data%20Specification%20v1.1.pdf) — field definitions and event semantics.
- [MIT DSpace record for Bush's paper](https://dspace.mit.edu/entities/publication/2e973612-e01f-4857-a455-983b1150f82b) — institutional metadata.
- [Dixon-Coles DOI](https://doi.org/10.1111/1467-9876.00065) — primary statistical model reference.

## Research-to-code protocol
For every source-driven change, record the claim and page/section, source type, required inputs, data available in UFI, falsifiable hypothesis, baseline, chronological test and uncertainty. Link cataloguing is not equivalent to reading every paper in full. Different sports, jurisdictions and promotions are not interchangeable.

## Model experiments
1. Compare independent Poisson with fitted Dixon-Coles/time decay on held-out chronological fixtures.
2. Test no-vig market probabilities against model calibration, with pre-kickoff timestamps and permitted provenance.
3. Add player evidence only after stable player-match joins and verified minutes.
4. Prototype pitch control only with legitimate tracking data and compare against simpler spatial baselines.
5. Keep aerodynamics as a research layer until measured trajectory/spin/environment data exists.
6. Keep Kelly/stake sizing out of production decisions until probability calibration and edge uncertainty are defensible.

# UFI slip-to-intelligence bridge

## What this build does

The browser app already uses Tesseract.js to extract text from one or more uploaded slip screenshots. This build adds a structured review pass over that text:

- identifies likely decimal odds tokens (1.01–1000);
- removes the extracted price from the displayed selection label;
- classifies common market wording (1X2, goals total, both teams to score, double chance, corners, cards, handicap, player and period markets);
- flags ambiguous rows, including lines with multiple decimal values;
- keeps raw OCR text and review notes alongside the parsed fields;
- exposes a CSV export for manual review and later fixture/market matching;
- avoids calculating combined odds when any saved selection has no valid price.

## Use

1. Open the UFI app and go to **Slip & odds lab**.
2. Upload one or more clear, full screenshots and select **Read text from photos (OCR)**.
3. Inspect the extracted text. Fix team names, market labels and odds in the text area if needed.
4. Select **Analyze slip** and inspect each parsed row and its confidence/review note.
5. Export **review CSV** if you want to inspect or process the structured rows outside the browser.
6. Remove bad rows or manually add corrected selections before saving the analysis.

## Important boundaries

- OCR confidence is a heuristic, not a calibrated probability that the row is correct.
- The market classifier recognizes wording only. It does not confirm the bookmaker's internal market ID, fixture identity, settlement rules or selection validity.
- Odds extraction looks for decimal-form odds. Integer-only prices are deliberately not guessed because integers can be scorelines, dates or market thresholds.
- The review CSV is not yet an input to the historical model/market audit. A later fixture-matching stage must resolve teams, competition, kickoff, market type and selection outcome; odds timestamps and permitted source provenance are also required before any historical market evaluation.
- Do not treat the bookmaker's implied probability as an independent model estimate. No edge or betting recommendation is generated by this OCR layer.
- Tesseract.js currently loads from a CDN, so OCR itself needs internet access. Manual text parsing and the rest of the static app can run after the app is loaded, but offline OCR requires bundling the library and language data locally.

## Export schema

`selection, market_guess, decimal_odds, implied_probability, independent_estimate, parse_confidence, correlation_group, warning, raw_line, source`

Empty or uncertain values should remain empty and be verified, not silently imputed.


## Fixture-candidate matching build

The app can now compare explicit “Team A vs Team B” text against the currently loaded StatsBomb competition-season match list. It normalizes common punctuation and club suffixes, matches the team pair in either orientation, and records a candidate match ID/date and home/away orientation only when one exact normalized pair is found. It distinguishes missing opponent, no loaded fixtures, no match in the selected list and ambiguous pairs. Slip rows with recognizable market wording or an explicit fixture can remain in the review even when OCR did not find a decimal price.

### Workflow

1. Load the competition, season and match list in the real-data section.
2. Analyze the slip text.
3. Select **Match selections to loaded fixtures**.
4. Review every candidate and export the updated review CSV.

A candidate is not an authoritative match. Verify competition, date, kickoff, team identity and bookmaker market/settlement rules manually. Single-team selections such as “Arsenal win” are intentionally not assigned an opponent. The current browser matcher does not guess unmentioned fixtures, use fuzzy nearest-name matches, or connect a market to model probabilities yet. This is a conservative first fixture-resolution layer.


## Scenario-to-market bridge build

A new optional **Compare supported markets with current scenario** action compares clearly recognized slip selections against the currently calculated independent-Poisson score grid. It requires a unique fixture candidate and exact agreement between the candidate teams and scenario teams, plus decimal odds. Supported examples are BTTS yes, over/under 1.5/2.5/3.5 goals, explicit home/draw/away result, and common double-chance wording. Unsupported or ambiguous selections remain unestimated rather than being guessed. It displays model probability, raw odds-implied probability, and estimated expected value (p \times odds - 1) for research review.

This is not a recommendation engine: the current scenario is a simplified baseline, not necessarily calibrated for the fixture. The exact market/settlement rule and candidate fixture still require human verification. EV estimates must not be treated as a proven edge without chronological out-of-sample validation, uncertainty checks, and correlation-aware analysis.


## Strict Slip Auditor
The consolidated gate checks OCR confidence, fixture resolution, supported market estimate, positive estimated EV, six current-context checks, source URL, written caveats, loaded lineup evidence and at least 30 locally saved settled forecasts. These are conservative operational gates, not validated universal thresholds. Current-news checks are user-attested; search links do not mean UFI fetched the page. Mark replacement candidates by row number and model them individually. Unqualified slots remain empty.


## Live-news and automatic cut feature
The Strict Slip Auditor now queries GDELT DOC 2.0 for recent articles for each uniquely matched fixture, displays source links and timestamps, flags injury/availability/rotation/lineup/congestion language, scores each selection, and separates CUT, HOLD and survivor candidates. The user can explicitly remove all CUT rows from the current slip. A failed feed or unresolved fixture is treated as unknown/high risk, not as clearance. Headline matching is heuristic and cannot verify reports; official club/league announcements and exact market settlement still require checking.

# UFI unified mathematics: football, human movement, probability and market odds

## Purpose

This is the research design for the next UFI intelligence layer: connect the user's football-physics ideas, player and tactical evidence, match probabilities, and the odds visible on uploaded betting slips. The objective is calibrated probability estimates and evidence-based price comparison—not a guaranteed-pick machine.

## One connected evidence-to-decision pipeline

1. **Read the slip, don't trust OCR blindly.** Extract fixture, market, selection, and decimal odds from each image. Keep the original image available for review, assign field-level OCR confidence, and require confirmation for uncertain teams, markets, or digits. Deduplicate repeated selections and resolve the exact fixture before analysis.
2. **Build the real match record.** Join historical results, event data, lineups, minutes, substitutions, player IDs, and team context only where source terms allow it. Preserve source, timestamp, competition, season, and missingness. Never pretend a results-only file contains player or tracking information.
3. **Represent the players and match state.** Where evidence exists, encode preferred/weak foot, role, observed style, minutes, substitute status, rest, opponent, scoreline, red cards, and tactical context. Keep real-world observations separate from eFootball labels such as “Fox in the Box” or “Orchestrator.” A label is not a verified fact unless its source supports that claim.
4. **Use physics where the data can identify it.** At event or tracking level, measurable features can include shot distance and angle, body/ball geometry, movement speed, acceleration, pressure, passing lanes, and keeper position. The goal mouth and pitch are geometric constraints. These features may help explain shot or event probabilities when reliable coordinates exist. They cannot be reconstructed from final scores alone, and Newtonian mechanics does not by itself predict a whole match.
5. **Estimate match outcomes with probabilistic models.** Establish simple baselines first, then compare calibrated goal models (Poisson or Dixon–Coles where appropriate), team-strength/Elo models, and player/event models. Combine models only when chronological validation shows a benefit; do not assume an arbitrary 50/50 blend is best. Monte Carlo sampling can turn estimated scoring distributions into scoreline, 1X2, totals, and both-teams-to-score probabilities.
6. **Treat “quantum-style maths” as a testable modelling idea, not magic.** UFI may experiment with probabilistic state representations, Bayesian updating, uncertainty distributions, and interactions between features. Quantum-inspired mathematics has no automatic predictive advantage for football; it must beat simpler models on held-out matches after accounting for complexity and calibration. Actual quantum computing is not required.
7. **Compare UFI with the bookmaker's price.** Convert decimal odds to raw implied probabilities p = 1 / odds; calculate market overround and normalize for a first-pass no-vig market probability. Compare UFI's calibrated probability with the market estimate and calculate expected return EV = p_UFI × decimal_odds − 1 for a one-unit stake, before fees, limits, and settlement rules. A positive estimated EV is a hypothesis, not proof of an edge. Use conservative uncertainty intervals and minimum evidence thresholds; if the evidence is weak, return “no bet / insufficient evidence.”
8. **Analyze the whole slip, not only each leg.** Estimate each selection's probability and odds, detect duplicated or conflicting markets, flag same-match correlation, calculate combined odds, and distinguish the naive product of leg probabilities from a model that accounts for dependence. If joint probabilities cannot be supported by data, clearly mark the combined estimate as unreliable rather than multiplying probabilities as if every leg were independent.
9. **Test in time order.** Freeze predictions before kickoff, use only features known at that timestamp, and evaluate untouched chronological periods. Report Brier score, log loss, calibration, sample size, per-league/market performance, uncertainty, and paper ROI only when complete, authorized, pre-kickoff odds and verified settlements exist. Compare against a market baseline. Keep every prediction so misses can be examined.
10. **Learn from results without fooling ourselves.** Update parameters only from settled, time-ordered records. Use ablation tests to learn whether footedness, substitutions, physics-derived features, or a new mathematical component actually improves forecasts. Avoid tuning repeatedly on the same test set.

## The user's high-goal scenario idea

The number of scoreline scenarios should not be chosen because a bigger number seems more powerful. Choose a goal grid from the training data's scoring distribution and validate tail coverage. For a Poisson rate lambda, the probability beyond a cap K is P(G > K) = 1 − sum(P(G = k), k = 0..K). Report this omitted tail mass. Increase the cap adaptively when tail mass exceeds a chosen tolerance, and test extreme-score coverage on held-out matches. A 15- or 20-goal cap is a computational boundary, not a way to force a match into a more likely result.

## Required evidence gates

- **Slip gate:** fixture and odds parsed; uncertain OCR reviewed; market rules identified.
- **Data gate:** source and permitted use recorded; team names and fixture joined unambiguously.
- **Timing gate:** every feature and odds quote existed before kickoff.
- **Player gate:** stable player IDs and match participation/minutes verified before player data affects predictions.
- **Physics gate:** coordinate definitions and tracking/event quality are known; no invented body or ball measurements.
- **Model gate:** calibration and performance tested out of sample against baselines.
- **Market gate:** odds margin, market type, correlation, settlement, and uncertainty handled.
- **Betting gate:** default output is a ranked research shortlist or “no bet,” never “sure bet.” Start with paper evaluation.

## Current implementation status

The repository already has a chronological Elo/Poisson backtest, a coordinated Phase 1–3 pipeline, a player evidence registry audit, odds timestamp checks, and a browser slip/odds workspace. This document specifies how those pieces should connect over time; it does not claim that screenshot OCR, complete player data, tracking physics, quantum-inspired models, or an automatic edge detector are already fully implemented. The public OpenFootball results pipeline remains results-only, so it cannot alone deliver the complete system described here.

## Immediate implementation order

1. Verify the newest integrated GitHub Actions run and fix any regressions.
2. Add a market-aware evaluation layer for user-supplied, authorized odds snapshots: no-vig probabilities, expected value, uncertainty, and comparisons to UFI forecasts.
3. Improve slip-image extraction with confidence and explicit user review, then map parsed selections to exact fixtures and markets.
4. Add suitable player-match data with stable IDs and pre-kickoff timestamps.
5. Add event-level geometry/physics features only when source data supports them.
6. Experiment with Bayesian or other quantum-inspired probability representations only as challengers to the simpler validated models.
7. Keep paper-trading results separate from real-world profit claims.


## Strict slip audit operational gates
The browser feature joins OCR confidence, unique fixture resolution and orientation, supported scenario mapping, estimated EV, current news/lineup/injury/form/rest/price attestations, a source URL, written caveats, lineup evidence and the local settled-forecast count. Current context is user-attested: the static app does not automatically verify news or lineups. It must not create three fresh alternatives without a separate verified fixture, odds and model estimate for each.

# Football-Data.co.uk source assessment for UFI

Source: https://football-data.co.uk/data  
Download index: https://football-data.co.uk/downloadm.php  
Reviewed: 2026-10-16

## Why this source matters

Football-Data.co.uk publishes historical match-level results, bookmaker odds and, for many competitions/seasons, basic match statistics in CSV/Excel form. Its published coverage includes the main English, Scottish, German, Italian, Spanish, French, Dutch, Belgian, Portuguese, Turkish and Greek divisions, plus extra leagues including Argentina, Austria, Brazil, China, Denmark, Finland, Ireland, Japan, Mexico, Norway, Poland, Romania, Russia, Sweden, Switzerland and the USA. Coverage differs by league and season.

Useful fields can include:
- Match date, home/away teams, full-time and half-time goals/results
- Bookmaker 1X2 odds and, where available, totals and Asian-handicap odds
- Shots, shots on target, corners, fouls, cards and referee fields in supported files/seasons
- Opening/pre-closing and closing odds in some seasons; coverage and column names vary

This is valuable for historical baselines, market comparison, scoreline modelling and chronological backtesting. It is **not** player-tracking data: it does not provide continuous player coordinates, exact sprint speeds, hip orientation, jump height, or complete ball trajectories.

## Important source-use restriction

The source's own data page states that the free spreadsheet data is intended for private individuals and not for commercial or data-training products using automated bots, scrapers or AI. UFI is an AI-assisted project, so do not bulk-download, scrape, train on, redistribute, or commit the source files to this public repository unless the source owner confirms that the intended use is permitted.

The public UFI repository must not contain copied bulk datasets from this source. The source links can be documented, and UFI can support data obtained under appropriate permission or terms. Contact Football-Data.co.uk to ask about permission/licensing for an AI-assisted, personal research/backtesting workflow before automating ingestion or model training.

## Integration requirements once permission is confirmed

1. Keep original files unchanged in a local, non-public data directory; do not commit them to Git.
2. Record source URL, retrieval date, league code, season, original filename and file hash in a manifest.
3. Treat each season/league as schema-variable. Never assume every file has the same columns.
4. Parse dates with explicit day/month handling and retain the original date value for audit.
5. Preserve missing values as missing; never fill absent shots, odds, referee or statistics with zero.
6. Keep bookmaker opening/pre-closing odds distinct from closing odds. Never use closing odds as a feature for a prediction that is meant to be made earlier.
7. Standardise team names with a documented alias table while retaining the original source name.
8. Validate duplicates, impossible scores, invalid odds and match-date/team mismatches before use.
9. Use chronological walk-forward evaluation; every feature must be available at the forecast timestamp. Fit transformations only on training data.
10. Report league/season coverage, missingness and source limitations in every evaluation.

## Suggested canonical match schema

- `source`, `source_url`, `retrieved_at`, `file_hash`
- `country`, `league_code`, `league_name`, `season`
- `match_date`, `kickoff_time`, `home_team_raw`, `away_team_raw`
- `home_goals_ft`, `away_goals_ft`, `full_time_result`
- `home_goals_ht`, `away_goals_ht`, `half_time_result`
- `home_shots`, `away_shots`, `home_shots_on_target`, `away_shots_on_target`
- `home_corners`, `away_corners`, `home_fouls`, `away_fouls`
- `home_yellows`, `away_yellows`, `home_reds`, `away_reds`, `referee`
- Separate columns for each odds provider and market, preserving source column names and whether odds are opening/pre-closing or closing.

Every field is optional by source/season and must carry missingness metadata. This schema is a target design, not a claim that every field exists in every file.

## Current implementation status

This document records source assessment and a safe integration plan only. No Football-Data.co.uk dataset has been copied into the UFI repository, and no downloader/scraper has been added. Ingestion and model training are blocked pending confirmation of permitted use.

# OpenFootball: free historical fixtures and results

Source: https://github.com/openfootball/football.json  
License: the upstream project dedicates its data and schema to the public domain; verify the current upstream `LICENSE.md` before redistributing a derived dataset.

## Why UFI uses it

OpenFootball provides season JSON files with match dates, home/away teams, fixtures and full-time scores where available. It needs no API key and is a useful base for historical form, scoreline frequencies, Elo/form features and chronological backtests.

It does **not** provide bookmaker odds, reliable player weak-foot labels, full player tracking or comprehensive event-level data. UFI combines it with separately licensed event data such as StatsBomb Open Data where appropriate.

## Download locally

From the repository root, with Python 3 installed and internet access:

```bash
python tools/openfootball_download.py
```

By default, this checks seasons from 2010/11 through 2026/27 across the league codes listed in the script. It saves raw JSON, a normalized `matches.csv`, and a provenance manifest under `data/private/openfootball/`. The output is git-ignored and is not uploaded to the public repository.

To start with only Premier League seasons:

```bash
python tools/openfootball_download.py --leagues en.1 --start-year 2010 --end-year 2025
```

To download a smaller test first:

```bash
python tools/openfootball_download.py --leagues en.1,de.1 --start-year 2022 --end-year 2023
```

The script skips missing season files (HTTP 404), logs other source/network errors, and records which source URL each row came from. Always inspect the manifest and missing values before modelling. It is a downloader for public-domain results—not a source for odds or tracking data.

# TheStatsAPI integration for UFI

Official site: https://www.thestatsapi.com/  
API docs: https://www.thestatsapi.com/docs  
Quickstart: https://www.thestatsapi.com/docs/quickstart  
Authentication: https://www.thestatsapi.com/docs/authentication  
Pagination: https://www.thestatsapi.com/docs/concepts/pagination

## Why this provider

The provider advertises fixtures/results, team and player data, match stats/events, xG, lineups, heatmaps, and odds across 150 competitions by default, with more available on request. Coverage varies by competition and season. Its website currently advertises a 7-day free trial and a Starter plan at $50/month; check current terms/pricing before starting. Sources: official pages linked above.

## Local downloader

`tools/thestatsapi_download.py` uses Python's standard library only. It downloads finished matches for a selected competition/season or a limited number of competitions, following the API's documented pagination format.

1. Create an account and API key via the provider's dashboard.
2. Set the key as an environment variable. Never paste it into source code or commit it:
   - Windows PowerShell (current terminal): `$env:THESTATSAPI_API_KEY="YOUR_KEY"`
   - macOS/Linux: `export THESTATSAPI_API_KEY="YOUR_KEY"`
3. Start with a single competition:
   `python tools/thestatsapi_download.py --search "Premier League"`
4. For a known competition ID and season ID:
   `python tools/thestatsapi_download.py --competition-id comp_3039 --season-id sn_8406098`
5. Only after checking your plan/quota, expand scope:
   `python tools/thestatsapi_download.py --all-competitions --max-competitions 5`

The output defaults to `data/private/thestatsapi/`, which is git-ignored. This avoids accidentally committing an API key or bulk provider data to the public UFI repo. Don't use `--all-competitions` without a scope/usage plan: every paginated request counts against API quotas, and "all seasons, all leagues" may require many requests.

## Important limitations

- The API key is not included in the repo; a user must create it.
- No API data has been downloaded or validated in this commit. The provider requires an authenticated key.
- This client fetches competition metadata and finished match records. It does not yet fetch odds, lineups, xG/event detail, player stats, or tracking data; those need dedicated endpoint adapters and validation.
- Match records are not tracking feeds. Do not infer exact sprint speeds, hip orientation, jump heights, or full ball trajectories unless a supported data source provides them.
- Keep data provenance, retrieval timestamps, missing values and original IDs. Verify licensing/retention/redistribution rights before publishing downloaded records.

# UFI Model Brain — research-grounded operating rules

Purpose: a traceable research guide for Universal Football Intelligence (UFI). This file is not a claim that all sources were fully accessible or that the app already implements every method. It separates established mechanics from features requiring data and validation.

## Operating doctrine

1. Evidence provenance first. Every input must be labelled public source, user-entered note, computed output, model estimate, or unknown. Record data timestamps, source URLs, market definitions and exact fixture ID. OCR text is provisional until checked.
2. Model before narrative. An LLM may summarize, challenge assumptions and list what to verify. It must not invent odds, lineups, injuries, player foot preference, weather, tracking coordinates, xG or probabilities. Its answer cannot turn HOLD into a cleared bet.
3. Fit and test. Fit attack/defence strengths on historical scorelines with time decay; compare independent Poisson, Dixon-Coles and simple league/home-away baselines. Test on later unseen matches. Track log loss, multiclass Brier, ranked probability score, calibration and performance by league/market. Do not optimize on the same test period used to report performance.
4. Probability and price. Decimal odds imply 1/odds before overround correction. For an exact market only, EV per unit stake is p*odds - 1. If p is uncalibrated, odds missing, settlement unclear, or market differs, EV is not established. Account for dependence in accumulators; multiplying leg probabilities assumes independence and is often wrong.
5. Physics is a conditional layer, not magic. Drag, Reynolds number, surface roughness, spin and Magnus effects affect a ball's trajectory. They do not directly determine match results. A useful shot-level physics model needs measured launch speed/angle/spin, ball properties, wind and trajectory/shot context plus outcome data. Without this, do not add a made-up physics score.
6. Spatial and biomechanical layer. Pitch control needs time-stamped player and ball coordinates, velocities, acceleration/reaction assumptions and team context. Event coordinates are sparse event locations, not full optical tracking. Dominant/weak foot, height/weight, style and substitute impact must be source-backed and time-relevant; missing data stays unknown.
7. Context and news. Recent form must be opponent-adjusted and split by venue with adequate sample size. Injuries/suspensions/lineups need recent reliable reports; search headlines (including GDELT) are leads, not confirmation. Schedule congestion and motivation should be explicit hypotheses, not guessed narrative.
8. Risk gate. Hard-stop only for verifiable contradictions (e.g. wrong/settled/historical fixture, invalid odds or impossible input). Unknown evidence, model disagreement, stale prices or unverified news should generally HOLD. “No red flag found” does not mean safe.
9. Replacement discipline. Replacement picks require a current fixture, exact market/settlement, verified price, model estimate for that market, independent baseline comparison and calibrated out-of-sample evidence. Otherwise show “NO QUALIFIED REPLACEMENT”; never fill a 3-leg ticket just to satisfy a format.
10. Bankroll. Kelly is a stake-sizing rule, not a match predictor. Use only after a robust positive edge is demonstrated; estimation error, correlated legs and bookmaker limits argue for conservative fractional sizing. Never present a staking formula as a way to guarantee profit.

## Source review register

The 24 URLs supplied by the project owner are preserved below. Pages and papers were opened where available on 2026-10-17; some links returned an internal error or maintenance page. Those entries remain references, not claims that their contents were fully reviewed. Prefer original academic papers and official bookmaker terms over SEO summaries.

### Fluid mechanics and ball aerodynamics

4. Goff & Chin, Bifurcation and Chaos in Trajectories of Knuckleballs and Smooth Spheres — https://arxiv.org/pdf/1710.02784
5. Asai et al., The Aerodynamics of Soccer Balls — https://arxiv.org/pdf/nlin/0611033
6. FlowThermoLab, Magnus Effect on a Rotating Soccer Ball — https://www.flowthermolab.com/magnus-effect-on-a-rotating-soccer-ball/

23. SportyBet Tanzania, bonuses and gifts help — https://www.sportybet.com/tz/m/help/how-to-play/others/bonuses-and-gifts
24. Bet9ja shop terms — https://shop.bet9ja.com/pages/sportfooter_terms/content

## Model implementation roadmap

- Added to the web UI: multi-provider AI evidence challenge; up to three providers called concurrently; configured fourth provider is sequential fallback; results remain advisory and are attached to the current risk-audit export.
- Not solved by adding LLMs: verified SportyBet live odds, official lineups/injury feed, complete event/tracking coverage, calibrated fixture-specific replacement predictions, or evidence that the model beats bookmaker prices after margin.
- Next quantitative step: create a reproducible historical training/evaluation pipeline, fit league-specific Dixon-Coles and simple baselines, store model version and data cutoff, produce out-of-sample calibration, and only then let exact-market probabilities enter EV/risk gating.


## Additional primary/adjacent sources discovered while following the supplied references

25. Dixon & Coles (1997), original peer-reviewed paper, *Modelling Association Football Scores and Inefficiencies in the Football Betting Market*, Journal of the Royal Statistical Society Series C, DOI 10.1111/1467-9876.00065 — https://rss.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/abs/10.1111/1467-9876.00065. This is the canonical source for the score model and historical market evaluation; its old-period reported returns are not evidence of current profitability.
26. Hudl StatsBomb Open Data official README — https://github.com/hudl/open-data/blob/master/README.md. Documents the free-data repository's competitions, matches, events, lineups, selected 360 data, and attribution expectations. The open dataset is selected coverage, not a universal live feed.
27. Kovalchik (2023), *Player Tracking Data in Sports*, Annual Review of Statistics and Its Application — https://doi.org/10.1146/annurev-statistics-033021-110117. Reviews high-resolution spatiotemporal tracking and analytics methods, with data/method limitations; supports the rule that player-level spatial claims require actual tracking data.

const assert = require('node:assert/strict');
const fs = require('node:fs');
const vm = require('node:vm');

const script = fs.readFileSync('ai-council.js', 'utf8');

function makeHarness({failGroq = false} = {}) {
  const elements = new Map();
  const calls = [];
  const attached = [];
  const get = id => {
    if (!elements.has(id)) {
      elements.set(id, {id, value:'', textContent:'', innerHTML:'', disabled:false, handlers:{},
        addEventListener(name, fn) { this.handlers[name] = fn; }});
    }
    return elements.get(id);
  };
  for (const [id,value] of Object.entries({
    aiGeminiKey:'gemini-test-key', aiGeminiModel:'gemini-test-model',
    aiGroqKey:'groq-test-key', aiGroqModel:'groq-test-model',
    aiOpenRouterKey:'router-test-key', aiOpenRouterModel:'router-test-model',
    aiHfKey:'hf-test-key', aiHfModel:'hf-test-model'
  })) get(id).value = value;
  const payload = {overall_status:'HOLD',summary:'Test-only advisory',traps:[],missing_evidence:['Test data only'],model_limitations:[],replacement_readiness:'NONE QUALIFIED',disagreements:[],questions_to_verify:[]};
  const okResponse = (data) => ({ok:true,status:200,text:async()=>JSON.stringify(data)});
  const context = {
    document:{getElementById:get},
    window:{UFI_AI_CONTEXT:()=>({testFixture:true, dataInventory:{eventsLoaded:0}}),UFI_ATTACH_AI_COUNCIL:r=>attached.push(r)},
    location:{origin:'https://test.invalid'},
    fetch:async (url,options)=>{
      calls.push({url,options});
      if(failGroq && url.includes('api.groq.com')) return {ok:false,status:429,text:async()=>JSON.stringify({error:{message:'rate limit test'}})};
      if(url.includes('generativelanguage.googleapis.com')) return okResponse({candidates:[{content:{parts:[{text:JSON.stringify(payload)}]}}]});
      return okResponse({choices:[{message:{content:JSON.stringify(payload)}}]});
    },
    AbortController, setTimeout, clearTimeout, Date, JSON, String, Number, Array, Object, Math, Error, Promise
  };
  vm.runInNewContext(script, context, {filename:'ai-council.js'});
  return {elements,get,calls,attached};
}

(async()=>{
  const normal=makeHarness();
  await normal.get('runAiCouncil').handlers.click();
  assert.equal(normal.calls.length,3,'three configured providers should run in parallel when all succeed');
  assert.equal(normal.attached[0].successfulModels,3,'all three parallel responses should be counted');
  assert.equal(normal.attached[0].fallbackUsed,null,'fallback should not run when three primary providers succeed');

  const failover=makeHarness({failGroq:true});
  await failover.get('runAiCouncil').handlers.click();
  assert.equal(failover.calls.length,4,'fourth provider should be called after one of the first three fails');
  assert.equal(failover.attached[0].successfulModels,3,'fallback should restore three successful model opinions');
  assert.equal(failover.attached[0].fallbackUsed,'Hugging Face','the configured spare provider should be recorded');
  assert.equal(failover.attached[0].models.find(m=>m.provider==='Groq').status,'failed','failed provider should be visible');
  console.log('AI council smoke tests passed: 3-way parallel execution and provider failover.');
})().catch(err=>{console.error(err);process.exitCode=1;});

const test = require('node:test');
const assert = require('node:assert/strict');
const fs = require('node:fs');
const vm = require('node:vm');

function boot() {
  const elements = new Map();
  const store = new Map();
  const get = id => {
    if (!elements.has(id)) elements.set(id, {
      id, value: '', innerHTML: '', textContent: '', className: '',
      handlers: {}, addEventListener(type, fn) { this.handlers[type] = fn; },
      click() { this.handlers.click?.(); }
    });
    return elements.get(id);
  };
  get('esiHome').value = 'Home';
  get('esiAway').value = 'Away';
  get('esiLambdaHome').value = '1.45';
  get('esiLambdaAway').value = '1.20';
  get('esiTopK').value = '5';
  const context = {
    document: { getElementById: get },
    localStorage: {
      getItem(k) { return store.get(k) ?? '[]'; },
      setItem(k, v) { store.set(k, v); }
    },
    URL: { createObjectURL() { return 'blob:test'; }, revokeObjectURL() {} },
    Blob: class Blob { constructor(parts) { this.parts = parts; } },
    confirm: () => true,
    Date, Math, Number, String, JSON, Array, Object, Set,
    console
  };
  vm.runInNewContext(fs.readFileSync('exact-score-intelligence.js', 'utf8'), context);
  return { get, store };
}

test('scoreline grid renders normalized 1X2 probabilities and scorelines', () => {
  const { get } = boot();
  get('esiCalculate').click();
  const html = get('esiSummary').innerHTML;
  assert.match(html, /Home win/);
  assert.match(html, /Draw/);
  assert.match(html, /Away win/);
  const firstThree = [...html.matchAll(/<b>([0-9]+\.[0-9]+)%<\/b>/g)].slice(0, 3).map(m => Number(m[1]));
  assert.equal(firstThree.length, 3);
  assert.ok(Math.abs(firstThree.reduce((a,b)=>a+b,0)-100) < 0.1);
  assert.match(get('esiGrid').innerHTML, /Probability/);
});

test('forecast snapshots persist separately in local storage', () => {
  const { get, store } = boot();
  get('esiCalculate').click();
  get('esiSaveForecast').click();
  const records = JSON.parse(store.get('ufi_exact_score_intelligence_v1'));
  assert.equal(records.length, 1);
  assert.equal(records[0].type, 'forecast');
  assert.equal(records[0].top5.length, 5);
  assert.ok(records[0].createdAt);
});

test('post-match audit permits unknown cause and preserves score evidence', () => {
  const { get, store } = boot();
  get('esiPostMatch').value = 'Test FC vs Sample FC';
  get('esiSelected').value = '2-1';
  get('esiFinal').value = '2-3';
  get('esiReason').value = 'unknown';
  get('esiSavePostmortem').click();
  const records = JSON.parse(store.get('ufi_exact_score_intelligence_v1'));
  assert.equal(records[0].type, 'postmortem');
  assert.equal(records[0].reason, 'unknown');
  assert.equal(records[0].selectedScore, '2-1');
  assert.equal(records[0].finalScore, '2-3');
});

function boot(fetchImpl=async()=>{throw Error('not configured')}){

 for(const [id,value] of Object.entries({lmsMinute:'70',lmsScoreH:'1',lmsScoreA:'0',lmsRemH:'0.4',lmsRemA:'0.3',lmsShockLimit:'10',lmsOddsSnapshots:'[]',lmsMinHistory:'3',lmsWindow:'5',lmsWeatherBase:'',lmsWeatherLat:'',lmsWeatherLon:'',lmsWeatherDate:'',lmsWeatherHour:'15'}))get(id).value=value;

function snapshots(a,b,first,second){
 return JSON.stringify([
 {timestamp:a,home:first[0],draw:first[1],away:first[2]},
 {timestamp:b,home:second[0],draw:second[1],away:second[2]}
 ]);
}
test('market shock pauses rather than force-fitting a large odds move',()=>{
 const g=boot(),now=Date.now();
 g('lmsOddsSnapshots').value=snapshots(new Date(now-12000).toISOString(),new Date(now-2000).toISOString(),[2.6,3.0,2.7],[1.65,3.5,4.8]);
 g('lmsRun').click();
 assert.match(g('lmsOutput').innerHTML,/PAUSE \/ DISCARD CURRENT FORECAST/);
 assert.match(g('lmsOutput').innerHTML,/Conditional score branches/);
});
test('stale odds are rejected',()=>{
 const g=boot(),now=Date.now();
 g('lmsOddsSnapshots').value=snapshots(new Date(now-240000).toISOString(),new Date(now-230000).toISOString(),[2.2,3.1,3.2],[2.18,3.1,3.25]);
 g('lmsRun').click();
 assert.match(g('lmsOutput').innerHTML,/DISCARD \/ HOLD/);
 assert.match(g('lmsOutput').innerHTML,/STALE/);
});
test('malformed or missing timestamped odds do not produce a forecast',()=>{

});
test('future-dated odds are rejected',()=>{
 const g=boot(),now=Date.now();

 g('lmsRun').click();
 assert.match(g('lmsOutput').innerHTML,/FUTURE-DATED/);
});

import unittest
from tools.exact_score_walkforward import evaluate, pmf, result_probs


class WalkForwardTests(unittest.TestCase):
    def test_poisson_distribution_starts_correctly(self):
        self.assertAlmostEqual(pmf(0, 0), 1.0)
        self.assertAlmostEqual(pmf(1, 0), 0.0)

    def test_outcomes_normalize(self):
        _, p = result_probs(1.4, 1.1)
        self.assertAlmostEqual(sum(p), 1.0, places=9)

    def test_predictions_start_after_minimum_history_and_compare_baselines(self):
        rows = []
        for i in range(30):
            rows.append({"date": f"2025-01-{(i % 28)+1:02d}", "sort": float(i),
                         "home_team": "A" if i % 2 == 0 else "B",
                         "away_team": "B" if i % 2 == 0 else "A",
                         "home_goals": 1 + (i % 3 == 0), "away_goals": i % 2})
        rows.sort(key=lambda r: r["sort"])
        report, predictions = evaluate(rows, min_history=10, team_window=5)
        self.assertEqual(len(predictions), 20)
        self.assertEqual(report["models"]["league_poisson"]["n"], 20)
        self.assertEqual(report["models"]["rolling_team_rates"]["n"], 20)
        self.assertIn("exact_score_logloss", predictions[0]["models"]["league_poisson"])


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()

import unittest
from unittest.mock import patch
import tools.ufi_live_data_proxy as proxy


class LiveProxyTests(unittest.TestCase):
    def test_health_does_not_need_token(self):
        with patch.object(proxy, "TOKEN", ""):
            data, _ = proxy.get_payload("/api/health", {})
        self.assertTrue(data["ok"])
        self.assertFalse(data["tokenConfigured"])

    def test_fixture_requires_numeric_id(self):
        with self.assertRaises(proxy.ProviderError):
            proxy.get_payload("/api/fixture", {"id": ["abc"]})

    def test_unknown_route_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaises(proxy.ProviderError):
            proxy.get_payload("/nope", {})

    def test_inplay_odds_route_filters_fixture_bookmaker_and_market(self):
        fixture, bookmaker, market = "12345", "2", "1"
        key = f"inplay-odds:{fixture}:{bookmaker}:{market}"
        old = proxy.CACHE.pop(key, None)
        try:
            expected = {"data": [{"label": "Home", "value": "2.0"}]}
            with patch.object(proxy, "provider_get", return_value=expected) as mocked:
                payload, cache_hit = proxy.get_payload("/api/inplay-odds", {
                    "id": [fixture], "bookmaker": [bookmaker], "market": [market]
                })
            self.assertFalse(cache_hit)
            self.assertEqual(payload["source"], "Sportmonks in-play odds")
            mocked.assert_called_once_with(
                "/odds/inplay/fixtures/12345",
                {"include": "market;bookmaker;fixture", "filters": "bookmakers:2;markets:1"}
            )
        finally:
            proxy.CACHE.pop(key, None)
            if old is not None:
                proxy.CACHE[key] = old

    def test_inplay_odds_requires_numeric_provider_ids(self):
        with self.assertRaises(proxy.ProviderError):
            proxy.get_payload("/api/inplay-odds", {"id": ["bad"], "bookmaker": ["2"], "market": ["1"]})

    def test_weather_route_validates_coordinates_and_date(self):
        with self.assertRaises(proxy.ProviderError):
            proxy.get_payload("/api/weather", {"lat": ["999"], "lon": ["0"], "date": ["2026-10-25"], "hour": ["12"]})
        with self.assertRaises(proxy.ProviderError):
            proxy.get_payload("/api/weather", {"lat": ["0"], "lon": ["0"], "date": ["not-a-date"], "hour": ["12"]})

    def test_weather_route_uses_open_meteo_adapter_and_returns_provenance(self):
        lat, lon, day, hour = 11.111, 22.222, "2026-10-25", 15
        key = f"weather:{lat:.3f}:{lon:.3f}:{day}:{hour}"
        old = proxy.CACHE.pop(key, None)
        try:
            expected = {"source": "Open-Meteo", "fetchedAt": 123.0, "weather": {"temperatureC": 25}}
            with patch.object(proxy, "open_meteo_weather", return_value=expected) as mocked:
                payload, cache_hit = proxy.get_payload("/api/weather", {
                    "lat": [str(lat)], "lon": [str(lon)], "date": [day], "hour": [str(hour)]
                })
            self.assertFalse(cache_hit)
            self.assertEqual(payload["source"], "Open-Meteo")
            self.assertEqual(payload["weather"]["temperatureC"], 25)
            mocked.assert_called_once_with(lat, lon, day, hour)
        finally:
            proxy.CACHE.pop(key, None)
            if old is not None:
                proxy.CACHE[key] = old


    def test_origin_allowlist_requires_exact_browser_origin(self):
        self.assertTrue(proxy.origin_allowed(None))
        self.assertTrue(proxy.origin_allowed(proxy.ALLOWED_ORIGIN))
        self.assertFalse(proxy.origin_allowed("https://attacker.example"))
        self.assertFalse(proxy.origin_allowed(proxy.ALLOWED_ORIGIN + ".evil.example"))

    def test_rate_limit_blocks_excess_requests_and_expires(self):
        key = "unit-test-client"
        old = proxy.REQUESTS.pop(key, None)
        old_limit = proxy.RATE_LIMIT
        try:
            proxy.RATE_LIMIT = 2
            self.assertFalse(proxy.rate_limited(key, now=100))
            self.assertFalse(proxy.rate_limited(key, now=101))
            self.assertTrue(proxy.rate_limited(key, now=102))
            self.assertFalse(proxy.rate_limited(key, now=100 + proxy.RATE_WINDOW + 1))
        finally:
            proxy.REQUESTS.pop(key, None)
            if old is not None:
                proxy.REQUESTS[key] = old
            proxy.RATE_LIMIT = old_limit

    def test_provider_url_uses_secret_but_error_does_not_echo_it(self):
        with patch.object(proxy, "TOKEN", "not-a-real-secret"), patch.object(
            proxy.urllib.request, "urlopen", side_effect=Exception("network")
        ):
            # Unexpected exceptions aren't normalized by the transport, but the
            # URL itself is not emitted by the function or request logger.
            self.assertTrue(proxy.TOKEN)
        self.assertNotIn("not-a-real-secret", proxy.API_BASE)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()

import sys
import unittest
from pathlib import Path

sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1] / "tools"))
import openfootball_backtest as backtest


class BacktestMathTests(unittest.TestCase):
    def test_outcome_probabilities_are_normalized(self):
        probs = backtest.outcome_probs(1.45, 1.1)
        self.assertEqual(len(probs), 3)
        self.assertTrue(all(0 <= p <= 1 for p in probs))
        self.assertAlmostEqual(sum(probs), 1.0, places=10)

    def test_score_metrics_are_finite_and_nonnegative(self):
        brier, logloss = backtest.scores_for((0.5, 0.25, 0.25), "H")
        self.assertGreaterEqual(brier, 0)
        self.assertGreaterEqual(logloss, 0)

    def test_invalid_goal_values_are_rejected(self):
        self.assertIsNone(backtest.safe_float(""))
        self.assertIsNone(backtest.safe_float("not-a-score"))
        self.assertIsNone(backtest.safe_float("-1"))
        self.assertEqual(backtest.safe_float("2"), 2.0)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()

import importlib.util, math, tempfile, unittest
from pathlib import Path

SCRIPT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1] / "tools" / "ufi_advanced_backtest.py"
spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location("ufi_advanced_backtest", SCRIPT)
model = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec); spec.loader.exec_module(model)

class AdvancedModelTests(unittest.TestCase):
    def test_elo_probabilities_sum_to_one(self):
        p = model.probs_from_ratings(1500, 1500)
        self.assertAlmostEqual(sum(p), 1.0, places=10)
        self.assertTrue(all(0 <= x <= 1 for x in p))
    def test_poisson_probabilities_sum_to_one(self):
        self.assertAlmostEqual(sum(model.poisson_probs(1.7)), 1.0, places=10)
    def test_poisson_1x2_sum_to_one(self):
        self.assertAlmostEqual(sum(model.poisson_1x2(1.4, 1.1)), 1.0, places=10)
    def test_scoring_finite(self):
        b, ll = model.score((0.5,0.25,0.25), "H")
        self.assertTrue(math.isfinite(b) and math.isfinite(ll))
        self.assertGreaterEqual(b, 0); self.assertGreaterEqual(ll, 0)

if __name__ == "__main__": unittest.main()

class IntegratedPipelineTests(unittest.TestCase):
 def test_empty_registry_is_not_misrepresented_as_player_data(self):
  with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:
   path=Path(tmp)/"players.csv"
   path.write_text("player_name,source_name,source_url,preferred_foot,weak_foot_evidence,role,observed_style_tags,efootball_style_tags,confidence_0_to_1,record_type\n",encoding="utf-8")
   result=pipeline.audit_player_registry(path)
   self.assertEqual(result["records"],0); self.assertFalse(result["used_as_prediction_feature"]); self.assertTrue(result["warnings"])
 def test_sourced_player_record_is_counted(self):
  with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:
   path=Path(tmp)/"players.csv"
   fields=["player_name","source_name","source_url","preferred_foot","weak_foot_evidence","role","observed_style_tags","efootball_style_tags","confidence_0_to_1","record_type"]
   with path.open("w",newline="",encoding="utf-8") as f:
    w=csv.DictWriter(f,fieldnames=fields); w.writeheader()
    w.writerow({"player_name":"Example Player","source_name":"Club profile","source_url":"https://example.com/player","preferred_foot":"left","weak_foot_evidence":"Observed on both feet across full matches","role":"winger","observed_style_tags":"wide carrier","efootball_style_tags":"none","confidence_0_to_1":"0.8","record_type":"source_fact"})
   result=pipeline.audit_player_registry(path)
   self.assertEqual(result["records"],1); self.assertEqual(result["footedness_known_records"],1); self.assertEqual(result["weak_foot_evidence_records"],1); self.assertEqual(result["style_tagged_records"],1); self.assertFalse(result["errors"])
 def test_invalid_foot_is_flagged(self):
  with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:
   path=Path(tmp)/"players.csv"
   path.write_text("player_name,source_name,source_url,preferred_foot,weak_foot_evidence,role,observed_style_tags,efootball_style_tags,confidence_0_to_1,record_type\nExample,source,https://example.com,maybe,,,,,0.5,source_fact\n",encoding="utf-8")
   result=pipeline.audit_player_registry(path)
   self.assertEqual(result["status"],"needs_correction"); self.assertTrue(result["errors"])

import importlib.util
import tempfile
import unittest
from pathlib import Path

SCRIPT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1] / "tools" / "ufi_probability_market_audit.py"
spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location("ufi_probability_market_audit", SCRIPT)
audit = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
spec.loader.exec_module(audit)


class ProbabilityMarketAuditTests(unittest.TestCase):
    def test_normalized_market_sums_to_one_and_reports_overround(self):
        probs, overround = audit.normalize_market([2.0, 3.5, 4.0])
        self.assertAlmostEqual(sum(probs), 1.0)
        self.assertGreater(overround, 0.0)

    def test_rejects_invalid_decimal_odds(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ValueError):
            audit.normalize_market([2.0, 1.0, 3.0])

    def test_rejects_timezone_missing_timestamp(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ValueError):
            audit.parse_timestamp("2026-10-16T12:00:00")

    def test_rejects_capture_after_kickoff(self):
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp:
            path = Path(temp) / "odds.csv"
            path.write_text(
                "date,league_code,home_team,away_team,home_odds,draw_odds,away_odds,captured_at_utc,kickoff_at_utc\n"
                "2026-10-01,ENG1,A,B,2.1,3.2,3.4,2026-10-01T20:00:00Z,2026-10-01T19:00:00Z\n",
                encoding="utf-8",
            )
            rows, rejected = audit.load_odds(path)
            self.assertEqual(rows, {})
            self.assertEqual(rejected["missing_or_invalid_timestamp"], 1)

    def test_duplicate_fixture_prices_are_excluded_even_with_three_rows(self):
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp:
            path = Path(temp) / "odds.csv"
            header = "date,league_code,home_team,away_team,home_odds,draw_odds,away_odds,captured_at_utc,kickoff_at_utc\n"
            row = "2026-10-01,ENG1,A,B,2.1,3.2,3.4,2026-10-01T17:00:00Z,2026-10-01T19:00:00Z\n"
            path.write_text(header + row + row + row, encoding="utf-8")
            rows, rejected = audit.load_odds(path)
            self.assertEqual(rows, {})
            self.assertEqual(rejected["duplicate_key"], 2)

    def test_end_to_end_audit_writes_probability_gap_and_scores(self):
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp:
            root = Path(temp)
            predictions = root / "predictions.csv"
            odds = root / "odds.csv"
            predictions.write_text(
                "date,league_code,home_team,away_team,actual_1x2,ensemble_p_home,ensemble_p_draw,ensemble_p_away\n"
                "2026-10-01,ENG1,A,B,H,0.5,0.3,0.2\n",
                encoding="utf-8",
            )
            odds.write_text(
                "date,league_code,home_team,away_team,home_odds,draw_odds,away_odds,captured_at_utc,kickoff_at_utc\n"
                "2026-10-01,ENG1,A,B,2.0,3.3333333333,5.0,2026-10-01T17:00:00Z,2026-10-01T19:00:00Z\n",
                encoding="utf-8",
            )
            summary = audit.run_audit(predictions, odds, root / "out")
            self.assertEqual(summary["matched_scored_fixtures"], 1)
            self.assertTrue((root / "out" / "probability_market_audit.csv").is_file())
            self.assertTrue((root / "out" / "probability_market_audit.json").is_file())
            with (root / "out" / "probability_market_audit.csv").open(encoding="utf-8-sig", newline="") as handle:
                import csv
                row = next(csv.DictReader(handle))
            self.assertIn("probability_gap_home", row)
            self.assertIn("brier_difference_model_minus_market", row)

    def test_brier_and_log_loss_are_finite_for_valid_forecast(self):
        probs = [0.5, 0.25, 0.25]
        self.assertGreaterEqual(audit.brier(probs, "H"), 0)
        self.assertGreaterEqual(audit.log_loss(probs, "H"), 0)

    def test_rejects_probabilities_that_do_not_sum_to_one(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ValueError):
            audit.brier([0.4, 0.4, 0.4], "H")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()

"""Chronological scoreline baseline evaluation; predictions use past matches only."""
import math

def pmf(k, rate):
    if rate == 0:
        return 1.0 if k == 0 else 0.0
    return math.exp(-rate + k * math.log(rate) - math.lgamma(k + 1))

def result_probs(home_rate, away_rate, cap=10):
    home = draw = away = 0.0
    exact = {}
    for h in range(cap + 1):
        for a in range(cap + 1):
            p = pmf(h, home_rate) * pmf(a, away_rate)
            exact[(h, a)] = p
            if h > a: home += p
            elif h == a: draw += p
            else: away += p
    total = home + draw + away
    return exact, (home / total, draw / total, away / total)

def evaluate(rows, min_history=100, team_window=10):
    past, team, predictions = [], {}, []
    for row in rows:
        if len(past) >= min_history:
            mean_h = sum(x["home_goals"] for x in past) / len(past)
            mean_a = sum(x["away_goals"] for x in past) / len(past)
            def rates(name):
                t = team.get(name, {"gf": [], "ga": []})
                n = len(t["gf"])
                base = max(.05, (mean_h + mean_a) / 2)
                return ((sum(t["gf"]) + 5*base)/(n+5)/base,
                        (sum(t["ga"]) + 5*base)/(n+5)/base)
            ha, hd = rates(row["home_team"]); aa, ad = rates(row["away_team"])
            models = {"league_poisson": (max(.05, mean_h), max(.05, mean_a)),
                      "rolling_team_rates": (max(.05, mean_h*ha*ad), max(.05, mean_a*aa*hd))}
            actual = (row["home_goals"], row["away_goals"])
            cls = 0 if actual[0] > actual[1] else (1 if actual[0] == actual[1] else 2)
            rec = {"date": row.get("date"), "home_team": row["home_team"], "away_team": row["away_team"],
                   "home_goals": actual[0], "away_goals": actual[1], "models": {}}
            for name, (lh, la) in models.items():
                exact, probs = result_probs(lh, la)
                rec["models"][name] = {"lambda_home": lh, "lambda_away": la,
                    "exact_score_logloss": -math.log(max(1e-12, exact.get(actual, 0))),
                    "one_x_two_logloss": -math.log(max(1e-12, probs[cls])),
                    "one_x_two_brier": sum((probs[i]-(i==cls))**2 for i in range(3)),
                    "p_home": probs[0], "p_draw": probs[1], "p_away": probs[2]}
            predictions.append(rec)
        past.append(row)
        for name, gf, ga in ((row["home_team"], row["home_goals"], row["away_goals"]),
                             (row["away_team"], row["away_goals"], row["home_goals"])):
            t = team.setdefault(name, {"gf": [], "ga": []})
            t["gf"] = (t["gf"] + [gf])[-team_window:]
            t["ga"] = (t["ga"] + [ga])[-team_window:]
    summary = {"models": {}}
    for name in ("league_poisson", "rolling_team_rates"):
        vals = [p["models"][name] for p in predictions]
        summary["models"][name] = {"n": len(vals)}
        for metric in ("exact_score_logloss", "one_x_two_logloss", "one_x_two_brier"):
            summary["models"][name][metric] = sum(v[metric] for v in vals)/len(vals) if vals else None
    summary["historical_matches"] = len(rows)
    summary["evaluated_unseen_matches"] = len(predictions)
    summary["note"] = "Lower scores are better. Chronological benchmark only; not proof of calibration or profit."
    return summary, predictions


def read_results_csv(path):
    import csv
    from datetime import datetime
    with open(path, newline="", encoding="utf-8-sig") as handle:
        reader = csv.DictReader(handle)
        required = {"date", "home_team", "away_team", "home_goals", "away_goals"}
        if not reader.fieldnames or not required.issubset(reader.fieldnames):
            raise ValueError("CSV columns required: date,home_team,away_team,home_goals,away_goals")
        rows = []
        for line, row in enumerate(reader, 2):
            try:
                stamp = datetime.fromisoformat(row["date"].strip().replace("Z", "+00:00")).timestamp()
                match = {"date": row["date"], "home_team": row["home_team"].strip(),
                         "away_team": row["away_team"].strip(), "home_goals": int(row["home_goals"]),
                         "away_goals": int(row["away_goals"]), "_timestamp": stamp}
                if not match["home_team"] or not match["away_team"] or min(match["home_goals"], match["away_goals"]) < 0:
                    raise ValueError()
                rows.append(match)
            except (ValueError, TypeError) as exc:
                raise ValueError("Invalid CSV row " + str(line)) from exc
    rows.sort(key=lambda item: item["_timestamp"])
    for row in rows:
        row.pop("_timestamp", None)
    return rows


def main():
    import argparse
    import json
    from pathlib import Path
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Chronological scoreline baseline evaluation")
    parser.add_argument("csv", help="historical results CSV")
    parser.add_argument("--min-history", type=int, default=100)
    parser.add_argument("--window", type=int, default=10)
    parser.add_argument("--out", default="exact-score-walkforward-report.json")
    args = parser.parse_args()
    summary, predictions = evaluate(read_results_csv(args.csv), max(1, args.min_history), max(1, args.window))
    summary["predictions"] = predictions
    Path(args.out).write_text(json.dumps(summary, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
    print(json.dumps({key: value for key, value in summary.items() if key != "predictions"}, indent=2))
    print("Full report written to " + args.out)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Chronological, same-date leakage-aware OpenFootball baseline backtest.

Uses only earlier calendar dates for all predictions on a date. Compares a
rolling venue-specific team-goal Poisson model with a smoothed league-outcome
frequency baseline. Python standard library only.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import csv
import json
import math
from collections import Counter, defaultdict, deque
from pathlib import Path


def poisson_probs(lam: float, max_goals: int = 8) -> list[float]:
    lam = max(0.05, min(6.0, lam))
    p = math.exp(-lam)
    probs = [p]
    for k in range(1, max_goals + 1):
        p = probs[-1] * lam / k
        probs.append(p)
    total = sum(probs)
    return [value / total for value in probs]


def outcome_probs(home_lam: float, away_lam: float) -> tuple[float, float, float]:
    hp, ap = poisson_probs(home_lam), poisson_probs(away_lam)
    home = draw = away = 0.0
    for i, p in enumerate(hp):
        for j, q in enumerate(ap):
            if i > j:
                home += p * q
            elif i == j:
                draw += p * q
            else:
                away += p * q
    total = home + draw + away
    return home / total, draw / total, away / total


def safe_float(value: object) -> float | None:
    try:
        if value is None or str(value).strip() == "":
            return None
        number = float(value)
        if not math.isfinite(number) or number < 0:
            return None
        return number
    except (TypeError, ValueError):
        return None


def scores_for(probs: tuple[float, float, float], actual: str) -> tuple[float, float]:
    labels = ("H", "D", "A")
    brier = sum((probs[i] - (1.0 if labels[i] == actual else 0.0)) ** 2 for i in range(3))
    logloss = -math.log(max(1e-12, probs[labels.index(actual)]))
    return brier, logloss


def summarize(records: list[dict], prefix: str) -> dict:
    if not records:
        return {"matches": 0, "accuracy_1x2": None, "mean_brier_1x2": None, "mean_logloss_1x2": None}
    return {
        "matches": len(records),
        "accuracy_1x2": round(sum(r[f"{prefix}_correct"] for r in records) / len(records), 6),
        "mean_brier_1x2": round(sum(r[f"{prefix}_brier"] for r in records) / len(records), 6),
        "mean_logloss_1x2": round(sum(r[f"{prefix}_logloss"] for r in records) / len(records), 6),
    }


def main() -> None:
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)
    parser.add_argument("--input", default="output/openfootball/matches.csv")
    parser.add_argument("--out", default="output/openfootball/model")
    parser.add_argument("--min-history", type=int, default=5)
    parser.add_argument("--window", type=int, default=10)
    args = parser.parse_args()
    if args.min_history < 1 or args.window < 1:
        parser.error("--min-history and --window must be positive integers")

    source, out = Path(args.input), Path(args.out)
    out.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    if not source.exists():
        raise SystemExit(f"Missing input CSV: {source}")
    with source.open(encoding="utf-8-sig", newline="") as handle:
        rows = list(csv.DictReader(handle))
    rows.sort(key=lambda r: (r.get("date", ""), r.get("league_code", ""), r.get("home_team", ""), r.get("away_team", "")))

    home_scored = defaultdict(lambda: deque(maxlen=args.window))
    home_conceded = defaultdict(lambda: deque(maxlen=args.window))
    away_scored = defaultdict(lambda: deque(maxlen=args.window))
    away_conceded = defaultdict(lambda: deque(maxlen=args.window))
    league_home_goals = defaultdict(lambda: deque(maxlen=100))
    league_away_goals = defaultdict(lambda: deque(maxlen=100))
    league_outcomes = defaultdict(Counter)
    predictions: list[dict] = []
    scored_records: list[dict] = []
    per_league: dict[str, list[dict]] = defaultdict(list)
    skipped_invalid = 0
    eligible_count = 0

    # Group all fixtures by date: every prediction for a date is made before
    # any result from that same date is added to the training history.
    by_date: dict[str, list[dict]] = defaultdict(list)
    for row in rows:
        by_date[row.get("date", "")].append(row)

    for date in sorted(by_date):
        todays_updates = []
        for row in by_date[date]:
            hg = safe_float(row.get("home_goals"))
            ag = safe_float(row.get("away_goals"))
            match_date = (row.get("date") or "").strip()
            league = (row.get("league_code") or "").strip()
            home_team = (row.get("home_team") or "").strip()
            away_team = (row.get("away_team") or "").strip()
            if (
                hg is None or ag is None or not hg.is_integer() or not ag.is_integer()
                or not match_date or not league or not home_team or not away_team
            ):
                skipped_invalid += 1
                continue
            hk, ak = (league, home_team), (league, away_team)
            actual = "H" if hg > ag else ("D" if hg == ag else "A")
            team_ready = (
                len(home_scored[hk]) >= args.min_history
                and len(away_scored[ak]) >= args.min_history
                and len(league_home_goals[league]) >= args.min_history
            )
            league_history_count = sum(league_outcomes[league].values())
            if team_ready and league_history_count >= args.min_history:
                base_h = sum(league_home_goals[league]) / len(league_home_goals[league])
                base_a = sum(league_away_goals[league]) / len(league_away_goals[league])
                h_attack = (sum(home_scored[hk]) / len(home_scored[hk])) / max(0.1, base_h)
                h_defence = (sum(home_conceded[hk]) / len(home_conceded[hk])) / max(0.1, base_a)
                a_attack = (sum(away_scored[ak]) / len(away_scored[ak])) / max(0.1, base_a)
                a_defence = (sum(away_conceded[ak]) / len(away_conceded[ak])) / max(0.1, base_h)
                home_lam = max(0.15, min(4.5, base_h * h_attack * a_defence))
                away_lam = max(0.15, min(4.5, base_a * a_attack * h_defence))
                model_probs = outcome_probs(home_lam, away_lam)

                # Laplace-smoothed league outcome baseline from prior dates only.
                counts = league_outcomes[league]
                denom = sum(counts.values()) + 3
                league_probs = tuple((counts[label] + 1) / denom for label in ("H", "D", "A"))
                model_brier, model_logloss = scores_for(model_probs, actual)
                base_brier, base_logloss = scores_for(league_probs, actual)
                model_pred = ("H", "D", "A")[max(range(3), key=lambda i: model_probs[i])]
                base_pred = ("H", "D", "A")[max(range(3), key=lambda i: league_probs[i])]
                rec = {
                    "date": date, "season": row.get("season", ""), "league_code": league,
                    "home_team": home_team, "away_team": away_team,
                    "actual_home_goals": int(hg), "actual_away_goals": int(ag),
                    "pred_home_goals": round(home_lam, 3), "pred_away_goals": round(away_lam, 3),
                    "p_home": round(model_probs[0], 6), "p_draw": round(model_probs[1], 6),
                    "p_away": round(model_probs[2], 6), "predicted_1x2": model_pred,
                    "actual_1x2": actual, "brier_1x2": round(model_brier, 6),
                    "logloss_1x2": round(model_logloss, 6),
                    "baseline_p_home": round(league_probs[0], 6),
                    "baseline_p_draw": round(league_probs[1], 6),
                    "baseline_p_away": round(league_probs[2], 6),
                    "baseline_predicted_1x2": base_pred,
                    "baseline_brier_1x2": round(base_brier, 6),
                    "baseline_logloss_1x2": round(base_logloss, 6),
                    "method": "rolling venue-specific goals; prior calendar dates only",
                }
                predictions.append(rec)
                score_record = {
                    "model_correct": int(model_pred == actual), "model_brier": model_brier,
                    "model_logloss": model_logloss, "baseline_correct": int(base_pred == actual),
                    "baseline_brier": base_brier, "baseline_logloss": base_logloss,
                }
                scored_records.append(score_record)
                per_league[league].append(score_record)
                eligible_count += 1

            todays_updates.append((league, hk, ak, hg, ag, actual))

        # Update training state only after all fixtures for this date were forecast.
        for league, hk, ak, hg, ag, actual in todays_updates:
            home_scored[hk].append(hg)
            home_conceded[hk].append(ag)
            away_scored[ak].append(ag)
            away_conceded[ak].append(hg)
            league_home_goals[league].append(hg)
            league_away_goals[league].append(ag)
            league_outcomes[league][actual] += 1

    pred_path = out / "chronological_predictions.csv"
    fields = list(predictions[0]) if predictions else [
        "date", "season", "league_code", "home_team", "away_team",
        "actual_home_goals", "actual_away_goals", "pred_home_goals", "pred_away_goals",
        "p_home", "p_draw", "p_away", "predicted_1x2", "actual_1x2",
        "brier_1x2", "logloss_1x2", "baseline_p_home", "baseline_p_draw",
        "baseline_p_away", "baseline_predicted_1x2", "baseline_brier_1x2",
        "baseline_logloss_1x2", "method"
    ]
    with pred_path.open("w", encoding="utf-8-sig", newline="") as handle:
        writer = csv.DictWriter(handle, fieldnames=fields)
        writer.writeheader()
        writer.writerows(predictions)

    per_league_metrics = {
        league: {
            "team_model": summarize(items, "model"),
            "league_frequency_baseline": summarize(items, "baseline"),
        }
        for league, items in sorted(per_league.items())
    }
    metrics = {
        "method": "chronological rolling venue-specific goals Poisson baseline",
        "data_source": "OpenFootball football.json",
        "matches_in_source_csv": len(rows),
        "backtested_matches": eligible_count,
        "rows_skipped_for_missing_or_invalid_fields": skipped_invalid,
        "min_history_per_team_venue": args.min_history,
        "rolling_window": args.window,
        "same_date_leakage_control": "All matches on a calendar date are forecast before any results from that date enter history; kickoff times are not available.",
        "team_model": summarize(scored_records, "model"),
        "league_frequency_baseline": summarize(scored_records, "baseline"),
        "per_league": per_league_metrics,
        "limitations": [
            "Baseline only; no odds, lineups, injuries, player traits or tracking.",
            "League outcome baseline is a smoothed prior-results benchmark, not bookmaker odds.",
            "Calendar dates are available but kickoff times are not; same-date results are withheld together.",
            "Metrics are not proof of betting profitability. Evaluate by season and against odds-based benchmarks where licensed data is available.",
            "Predictions are estimates, not guaranteed outcomes."
        ],
        "predictions_csv": str(pred_path),
    }
    metrics_path = out / "metrics.json"
    metrics_path.write_text(json.dumps(metrics, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
    print(json.dumps(metrics, indent=2))
    if eligible_count == 0:
        raise SystemExit("No eligible matches for backtesting. Check score parsing and data coverage.")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
